\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}
,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
and
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
functions. Define
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
to be the sentence

\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Y}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|\geq g(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}).

For the rest of this section, let
W
be the set of
\mathcal{L}_{R}
-sentences of the form
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
and let
V
be the set of
w\in W
such that there exists
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
with
M\models w
. If
X
and
Y
are both empty then
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
should be read as the true sentence.

Define
\mathrm{clx}_{1}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
to be

\left|\{\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\in X\ |\ f(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})>1\}\right|+\left|\{\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\in Y\ |\ g(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})>1\}\right|

and

\mathrm{clx}_{2}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
to be

\displaystyle\left|\{\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\in X\ |\ f(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})>1\text{ and }c,b\neq 0\}\right|+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left|\{\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\in Y\ |\ g(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})>1\text{ and }b,c\neq 0\}\right|.

Formally, we extend
\mathrm{clx}_{1}
and
\mathrm{clx}_{2}
to
\bot,\top\in\mathbb{W}
by setting
\mathrm{clx}_{1}\bot=\mathrm{clx}_{1}\top=0
and
\mathrm{clx}_{2}\bot=\mathrm{clx}_{2}\top=0
. We will use the notation
\mathrm{clx}_{i}\underline{w}\leq\mathrm{clx}_{i}w
and
\mathrm{clx}_{i}\underline{w}<\mathrm{clx}_{i}w
, for
i\in\{1,2\}
,
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
and
w\in W
as in subsection
4.2
.

Remark 5.4
.

For all
w_{1},w_{2}\in W
and
i\in\{1,2\}
,

\mathrm{clx}_{i}(w_{1}\wedge w_{2})\leq\mathrm{clx}_{i}(w_{1})+\mathrm{clx}_{i}(w_{2}).

For our purposes, given
w\in W
, we may always assume that
w
is of the form

{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi,

where
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}

with
X,Y
finite disjoint sets of pp-pairs of the form
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}
or
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}
,
and
\Xi
is an auxiliary sentence.

It is obvious that any
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\in W
may be rewritten as
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f^{\prime},g}\wedge\Xi
where
f^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
and
\Xi
is an auxiliary sentence. Moreover, for
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi\in W
, let
Y^{\prime}:=\{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\ |\ g(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})>1\}
and
g^{\prime}:=g|_{Y^{\prime}}
. Then
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi\in V
if and only if
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g^{\prime}}\wedge\Xi\in V
. If
\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X\cap Y
and
f(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})<g(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})
then
T_{R}\models\lnot{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
. If
f(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\geq g(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})
then
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi\in V
if and only if
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g^{\prime}}\wedge\Xi\in V
where
g^{\prime}:=g|_{Y\backslash\{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\}}
. So, given
w\in W
, we can effectively decide that
w\notin V
or compute
f,g
and
\Xi
of the required form such that,
\mathrm{clx}_{1}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi\leq\mathrm{clx}_{1}w
,
\mathrm{clx}_{2}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi\leq\mathrm{clx}_{2}w
, and,
w\in V
if and only if
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi\in V
.

Remark 5.5
.

Let
X,Y
be disjoint finite sets of pp-pairs of the form
d|x/x=0
or
xb=0/c|x
,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
and
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
functions, and
\Xi
an auxiliary sentence. For each
1\leq j\leq n
, let
\theta_{j}
be an auxiliary sentence. Suppose that for all
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
, there exist modules
M_{i}\models\theta_{j}
such that
M\equiv\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}M_{j}
. Then
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi\in V
if and only if

\bigsqcup_{(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{(f,g,n)}}\bigsqcap_{j=1}^{n}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{j},g_{j}}\wedge\theta_{j}\wedge\Xi\in\mathbb{V}.

Moreover, for all
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,n}
and
1\leq j\leq n
,

\ \ \ \ \ \ \mathrm{clx}_{1}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{j},g_{j}}\wedge\theta_{j}\wedge\Xi)\leq\mathrm{clx}_{1}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi)\text{ and }

\mathrm{clx}_{2}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{j},g_{j}}\wedge\theta_{j}\wedge\Xi)\leq\mathrm{clx}_{2}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi).

The next lemma is more precise than we will need in this section. However, we will need its full strength in section
9
. The total order
\prec
on the set
\{\emptyset,=,\leq\}
is defined as
\emptyset\,\prec\,=\,\prec\,\leq
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\varphi/\psi,\varphi^{\prime}/\psi^{\prime},\sigma/\tau
be pp-pairs,
\square,\square^{\prime}\in\{=,\geq\}
,
E,E^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{2}
and let
\Sigma
be an
\mathcal{L}_{R}
-sentence. Suppose that
M\models\Sigma
implies

|\varphi/\psi(M)|=|\sigma/\tau(M)|\cdot|\varphi^{\prime}/\psi^{\prime}(M)|

for all
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
.

There is an algorithm which, given
\varphi/\psi,\varphi^{\prime}/\psi^{\prime},\sigma/\tau
,
\square,\square^{\prime}\in\{=,\geq\}
,
E,E^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{2}
and
\Sigma
as above, either returns
\Omega:=\{\bot\}
, in which case

T_{R}\models\lnot(\Sigma\wedge|\varphi/\psi|\square E\wedge|\varphi^{\prime}/\psi^{\prime}|\square^{\prime}E^{\prime}),

or, returns
\Omega
, a finite set of tuples
(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\times\{=,\geq\}^{2}
such that

T_{R}\models\Sigma\wedge|\varphi/\psi|\square E\wedge|\varphi^{\prime}/\psi^{\prime}|\square^{\prime}E^{\prime}\leftrightarrow\bigvee_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})\in\Omega}\Sigma\wedge|\tau/\sigma|\square_{1}D_{1}\wedge|\varphi^{\prime}/\psi^{\prime}|\square_{2}D_{2}

and
D_{1}\cdot D_{2}<E\cdot E^{\prime}
,
\square_{1}\preceq\square
and
\square_{2}\preceq\square^{\prime}
for all
(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})\in\Omega
.

Proof.

Case 1:

\square
and
\square^{\prime}
are both
=
.

Let
\Omega:=\{\bot\}
if
E^{\prime}
does not divide
E
, otherwise
\Omega:=\{(E/E^{\prime},E^{\prime},=,=)\}
. Note
(E/E^{\prime})\cdot E^{\prime}=E<E\cdot E^{\prime}
because
E^{\prime}\geq 2
.

Case 2:

\square
is
\geq
and
\square^{\prime}
is
=
.

Let
\Omega:=\{(\lceil E/E^{\prime}\rceil,E^{\prime},\geq,=)\}
. Note

\lceil E/E^{\prime}\rceil\cdot E^{\prime}<(E/E^{\prime}+1)\cdot E=E+E^{\prime}\leq E\cdot E^{\prime}.

Case 3:

\square
is
=
and
\square^{\prime}
is
\geq
.

Let
X:=\{D\in\mathbb{N}\ |\ D|E\text{ and }D\geq E^{\prime}\}
. Define
\Omega:=\{\bot\}
if
X=\emptyset
and
\Omega:=\{(E/D,D,=,=)\ |\ D\in X\}
.
Note
(E/D)\cdot D=E<E\cdot E^{\prime}
.

Case 4:

\square
and
\square^{\prime}
are both
\geq
.

If
E^{\prime}\geq E
then let
\Omega:=\{(1,E^{\prime},\geq,\geq)\}
. If
E>E^{\prime}
then set

\Omega:=\{(\lceil E/D\rceil,D,\geq,=)\ |\ E>D\geq E^{\prime}\}\cup\{(1,E,\geq,\geq)\}.

Note that
E^{\prime}<E\cdot E^{\prime}
,
E<E\cdot E^{\prime}
and

\lceil E/D\rceil\cdot D<(E/D+1)\cdot D=E+D\leq E\cdot E^{\prime}.\qed

The following remark is easy to prove. We record it here because we will use it frequently.

Remark 5.7
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring. For all
a,b\in R
and
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
,

\left|\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{ab|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{xa=0+b|x}}(M)\right|

and

\left|\nicefrac{{xab=0}}{{xa=0}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{a|x\wedge xb=0}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
R
be a recursive arithmetical ring.
There is an algorithm which given
w\in W
with
\mathrm{clx}_{1}(w)>1
outputs
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
such that
\mathrm{clx}_{1}(\underline{w})<\mathrm{clx}_{1}(w)
,
\mathrm{clx}_{2}(\underline{w})\leq\mathrm{clx}_{2}(w)
, and,
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
.

Proof.

Let
X,Y
be disjoint finite sets of pp-pairs of the form
d|x/x=0
and
xb=0/c|x
. Let
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
and
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
be functions, and let
\Xi
be an auxiliary sentence. Let
w
be

\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Y}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|\geq g(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\Xi.

Suppose that there exist non-equal
a,b\in R
such that
\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}},\nicefrac{{b|x}}{{x=0}}\in X\cup Y
i.e.
\mathrm{clx}_{1}(w)>1
. Let
\alpha,r,s\in R
be such that
a\alpha=br
and
b(\alpha-1)=as
. Define

(1)

\Sigma_{1}:=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\alpha|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{rb|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1
,

(2)

\Sigma_{2}:=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\alpha|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{r|x}}\right|=1
,

(3)

\Sigma_{3}:=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{(\alpha-1)|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{as|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1
, and

(4)

\Sigma_{4}:=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{(\alpha-1)|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xa=0}}{{s|x}}\right|=1
.

It follows directly from
2.9
that, for any
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
, there are
M_{i}\models\Sigma_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq 4
such that
M\equiv M_{1}\oplus M_{2}\oplus M_{3}\oplus M_{4}
. Therefore, by
5.5
,
w\in V
if and only if

\bigsqcup_{(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,4}}\bigsqcap_{i=1}^{4}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{i}\in\mathbb{V}.

For each
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,4}
and
1\leq i\leq 4
, it is enough to compute
\underline{w}_{i}\in\mathbb{W}
such that
\mathrm{clx}_{1}(\underline{w}_{i})<\mathrm{clx}_{1}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi)
,
\mathrm{clx}_{2}(\underline{w}_{i})\leq\mathrm{clx}_{2}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi)
and
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{i}\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}_{i}\in V
.

Fix
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,4}
. For each
1\leq i\leq 4
, let
X_{i}
be the domain of
f_{i}
and
Y_{i}
be the domain of
g_{i}
.

Case i=1:
Suppose
M\models\Sigma_{1}
. Then
M\alpha=M
and hence
Ma=Mbr=0
. Therefore, if
M\models\Sigma_{1}
then
\left|\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|=1
.

If
\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}\in X_{1}
and
f_{1}(\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})=1
then
\mathrm{clx}_{1}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{1})<\mathrm{clx}_{1}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi)
and, by
5.5
,
\mathrm{clx}_{2}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{1})\leq\mathrm{clx}_{2}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi)
. So,
\underline{w}_{i}:={\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{1}
has the required properties.

If
\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}\in X_{1}
and
f_{1}(\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})\neq 1
then, by the first paragraph,
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{1}
is not satisfied by any
R
-module. If
\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}\notin X_{1}
then
\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}\in Y_{1}
since
X\cup Y=X_{1}\cup Y_{1}
. Moreover
g_{1}(\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})=g(\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})
. So
g_{1}(\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})>1
and hence
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{1}
is not satisfied by any
R
-module. In either case, set
\underline{w}_{i}:=\bot
. Then
\underline{w}_{i}\in\mathbb{V}
if and only if
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{1}\in V
. By definition
\mathrm{clx}_{1}(\bot)<\mathrm{clx}_{1}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi)
and
\mathrm{clx}_{2}(\bot)\leq\mathrm{clx}_{2}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi)
.

Case i=2:
Suppose
M\models\Sigma_{2}
. Then
Ma=M\alpha a=Mbr
. So, since
xb=0\leq_{M}r|x
, by
5.7
,

\left|\nicefrac{{b|x}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{b|x}}{{br|x}}(M)\right|\cdot\left|\nicefrac{{br|x}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{r|x}}(M)\right|\cdot\left|\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|.

Let
X^{\prime}=X_{2}\backslash\{\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}},\nicefrac{{b|x}}{{x=0}}\}
and
Y^{\prime}=Y_{2}\backslash\{\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}},\nicefrac{{b|x}}{{x=0}}\}
. Let
\square,\square^{\prime}\in\{=,\geq\}
and
A,B
be such that
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2}
is

\left|\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\square A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{b|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\square^{\prime}B\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X^{\prime}}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f_{2}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Y^{\prime}}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|\geq g_{2}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2}.

By
5.6
, there is an algorithm which either returns
\Omega:=\{\bot\}
, in which case

T_{R}\models\lnot\Sigma_{2}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\square A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{b|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\square^{\prime}B,

or, a set
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{N}^{2}\times\{=,\geq\}^{2}
such that

\Sigma_{2}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\square A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{b|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\square^{\prime}B

is equivalent, with respect to
T_{R}
, to

\bigvee_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})\in\Omega}\Sigma_{2}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{r|x}}\right|\square_{1}D_{1}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\square_{2}D_{2}.

If
\Omega:=\{\bot\}
then
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2}\in V
if and only if
\bot\in\mathbb{V}
. By definition
\mathrm{clx}_{1}(\bot)<\mathrm{clx}_{1}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi)
and
\mathrm{clx}_{2}(\bot)\leq\mathrm{clx}_{2}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi)
. Otherwise, for each
(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})\in\Omega
, let
u_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})}
be

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{r|x}}\right|\square_{1}D_{1}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\square_{2}D_{2}\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X^{\prime}}\!\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f_{2}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Y^{\prime}}\!\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|\geq g_{2}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2}.

Then
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2}
is equivalent to

\bigvee_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})\in\Omega}u_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})}

with respect to
T_{R}
. Therefore
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2}\in V
if and only if

\bigsqcup_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})\in\Omega}u_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})}\in\mathbb{V}.

Moreover,

\mathrm{clx}_{1}(u_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})})\leq 1+(\mathrm{clx}_{1}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2})-2)<\mathrm{clx}_{1}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2})

and
\mathrm{clx}_{2}(u_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})})=\mathrm{clx}_{2}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2})
.
So we are done, since, by
5.5
,
\mathrm{clx}_{1}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2})\leq\mathrm{clx}_{1}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi)
and
\mathrm{clx}_{2}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2})\leq\mathrm{clx}_{2}({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi)
.

The case
i=3
is similar to
i=1
and the case
i=4
is similar to
i=2
.
∎

We now start work on showing that there exists an algorithm which given
w\in W
with
\mathrm{clx}_{2}w>1
outputs
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
such that
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
and
\mathrm{clx}_{2}(\underline{w})<\mathrm{clx}_{2}(w)
. This uses the same ideas as for
\mathrm{clx}_{1}
but is significantly more complicated.

\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}
\Lambda_{1}
\Lambda_{2}
\Lambda^{\prime}_{2}
\Lambda_{3}
\Lambda^{\prime}_{3}
\Lambda^{\prime}_{4}
\Lambda_{4}
P^{\prime}_{4}

\Lambda_{5}

\left|\frac{x=x}{\alpha|x}\right|=1
\left|\frac{x=x}{\alpha-1|x}\right|=1
\left|\frac{c^{\prime}u|x}{x=0}\right|=1
\left|\frac{cu^{\prime}|x}{x=0}\right|=1
\left|\frac{xu=0}{c^{\prime}|x}\right|=1
\left|\frac{xu^{\prime}=0}{c|x}\right|=1
\left|\frac{x(\beta-1)=0}{x=0}\right|=1
\left|\frac{x(\beta^{\prime}-1)=0}{x=0}\right|=1
\left|\frac{x\beta=0}{x=0}\right|=1

\left|\frac{us|x}{x=0}\right|=1

\left|\frac{xs^{\prime}=0}{u^{\prime}|x}\right|=1
\left|\frac{x\delta=0}{x=0}\right|=1

\left|\frac{x(\delta-1)=0}{x=0}\right|=1
\left|\frac{x(\delta^{\prime}-1)=0}{x=0}\right|=1

\left|\frac{xb^{\prime}=0}{cu^{\prime}|x}\right|=1
\left|\frac{c^{\prime}bu|x}{x=0}\right|=1
\left|\frac{cb^{\prime}u^{\prime}|x}{x=0}\right|=1
\left|\frac{cu^{\prime}s^{\prime}|x}{x=0}\right|=1
\left|\frac{c^{\prime}us|x}{x=0}\right|=1
\left|\frac{\lambda s|x}{x=0}\right|=1
\left|\frac{\lambda^{\prime}s^{\prime}|x}{x=0}\right|=1

\left|\frac{x\lambda^{\prime}=0}{s^{\prime}|x}\right|=1
\left|\frac{b\mu|x}{x=0}\right|=1
\left|\frac{b^{\prime}\mu^{\prime}|x}{x=0}\right|=1
\left|\frac{x\mu=0}{b|x}\right|=1
\left|\frac{x\mu^{\prime}=0}{b^{\prime}|x}\right|=1

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
R
be an arithmetical ring and let
b,c,b^{\prime},c^{\prime}\in R
. Let
\alpha,u,u^{\prime},\allowbreak\beta,\beta^{\prime},s,\allowbreak s^{\prime},\allowbreak r\allowbreak,r^{\prime},\allowbreak\delta,\delta^{\prime},\lambda,\lambda^{\prime},\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in R
be such that

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccc}&&&&c\alpha&\!\!\!\!=&c^{\prime}u,&&c^{\prime}(\alpha-1)&\!\!\!\!=&cu^{\prime}&&&&\\
u\beta&\!\!\!\!=&b^{\prime}r,&&b^{\prime}(\beta-1)&\!\!\!\!=&us,&&u^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}&\!\!\!\!=&br^{\prime},&&b(\beta^{\prime}-1)&\!\!\!\!=&u^{\prime}s^{\prime}\\
b\delta&\!\!\!\!=&s\lambda,&&s(\delta-1)&\!\!\!\!=&b\mu,&&b\delta^{\prime}&\!\!\!\!=&s^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime},&&s^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime}-1)&\!\!\!\!=&b^{\prime}\mu^{\prime}.\end{array}

Define the sentences
\Lambda_{i}
and
\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}
for
1\leq i\leq 6
and
P_{i}
and
P_{i}^{\prime}
for
4\leq i\leq 6
to be the conjunction of sentences labeling the edges in the path from the root of the tree in Figure 1 to the leaf of the tree with that sentence as label.

Every
R
-module is elementary equivalent to an
R
-module of the form

(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{6}M_{i}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=4}^{6}N_{i})\oplus(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{6}M_{i}^{\prime}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=4}^{6}N_{i}^{\prime})

where
M_{i}\models\Lambda_{i}
and
M_{i}^{\prime}\models\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}
for
1\leq i\leq 6
and
N_{i}\models P_{i}
and
N_{i}^{\prime}\models P_{i}^{\prime}
for
4\leq i\leq 6
.

Moreover, for all
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
,

(1)

M\models\Lambda_{1}
implies
c\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}M
and hence
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|
,

(2)

M\models\Lambda_{2}
implies
\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}(M)\right|=1
,

(3)

M\models\Lambda_{3}
implies
b^{\prime}\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}M
and hence
\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}(M)\right|
,

(4)

M\models\Lambda_{4}
implies
\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}(M)\right|=1
,

(5)

M\models\Lambda_{5}
implies
b\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}M
and hence
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(M)\right|
,

(6)

M\models\Lambda_{6}
implies

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}(M)\right|\cdot\left|\nicefrac{{x\lambda=0}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|,

(7)

M\models P_{4}
implies
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(M)\right|=1,

(8)

M\models P_{5}
implies
b^{\prime}\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}M
and hence
\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}(M)\right|,
and

(9)

M\models P_{6}
implies

\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(M)\right|\cdot\left|\nicefrac{{x\mu=0}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|.

Similarly, the symmetry of figure 1, gives 9 conditions for
\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}
and
P_{i}^{\prime}
, where
c,b
and
c^{\prime},b^{\prime}
are interchanged
and
\lambda,\mu
are replaced by
\lambda^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}
, respectively.

Proof.

There are two edges coming out of each node of the tree in Figure
1
. In each instance the two edges are either

(1)

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\gamma|x}}\right|=1
and
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{(\gamma-1)|x}}\right|=1
for some
\gamma\in R
,

(2)

\left|\nicefrac{{x\gamma=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=1
and
\left|\nicefrac{{x(\gamma-1)=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=1
for some
\gamma\in R
, or

(3)

\left|\nicefrac{{ab|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1
and
\left|\nicefrac{{xa=0}}{{b|x}}\right|=1
.

By
2.9
, in each
(1),(2)
and
(3)
, for all modules
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
, there exist
M_{1}
satisfying the first sentence and
M_{2}
satisfying the second sentence such that
M\cong M_{1}\oplus M_{2}
. The first claim follows from this fact.

For any
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
,
\alpha\notin\textnormal{Div}M
implies
c|x
is equivalent to
c^{\prime}u|x
in
M
because
c\alpha=c^{\prime}u
.

(i)
Suppose
M\models\Lambda_{1}
. Since
\left|\nicefrac{{c^{\prime}u|x}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|=1
,
Mc=Mc^{\prime}u=0
. So
c\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}M
.

(ii)
Suppose
M\models\Lambda_{2}
. Then
\beta\notin\textnormal{Ass}M
and so, since
u\beta=b^{\prime}r
,
xb^{\prime}r=0
is equivalent to
xu=0
in
M
. Since
xu=0\leq_{M}c^{\prime}|x
, we conclude

xb^{\prime}=0\leq_{M}xb^{\prime}r=0\leq_{M}xu=0\leq_{M}c^{\prime}|x.

(iii)
Suppose
M\models\Lambda_{3}
. Then
\beta-1\notin\textnormal{Ass}M
and so, since
b^{\prime}(\beta-1)=us
,
xb^{\prime}=0
is equivalent to
xus=0
in
M
. Since
\left|\nicefrac{{us|x}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|=1
,
us\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}M
and hence
b^{\prime}\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}M
.

Claim:
If

M\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\alpha|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xu=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x(\beta-1)=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xs=0}}{{u|x}}\right|=1
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{xs=0}}{{c^{\prime}u|x}}(M)\right|
.

First note that since
\beta-1\notin\textnormal{Ass}M
and
b^{\prime}(\beta-1)=us
,
xb^{\prime}=0
is equivalent to
xus=0
in
M
. We show that
\left|\nicefrac{{xus=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{xs=0}}{{c^{\prime}u|x}}(M)\right|
.

Consider the map
f:xus=0/x=0(M)\rightarrow xs=0/c^{\prime}u|x(M)
defined by
f(m):=mu+c^{\prime}u|x(M)
. Now
f
is surjective since
\left|\nicefrac{{xs=0}}{{u|x}}\right|=1
. Suppose
f(m)=m^{\prime}c^{\prime}u
for some
m^{\prime}\in M
. Then
(m-m^{\prime}c)u=0
. Since
\left|\nicefrac{{xu=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\right|=1
,
m-m^{\prime}c\in c^{\prime}|x(M)
and hence
m\in c^{\prime}|x(M)
. Therefore
\ker f=c^{\prime}|x(M)
. So we have proved the claim.

We now prove the statements about modules satisfying
\Lambda_{4},\Lambda_{5},\Lambda_{6}
. The statements for modules satisfying
P_{4},P_{5}
and
P_{6}
follow similarly.

(iv)
Suppose
M\models\Lambda_{4}
. Then
\left|\nicefrac{{xs=0}}{{c^{\prime}u|x}}(M)\right|=1
and by the claim
\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{xs=0}}{{c^{\prime}u|x}}(M)\right|
.

(v)
Suppose
M\models\Lambda_{5}
. Since
\delta\notin\textnormal{Ass}M
,
xb=0
is equivalent to
xs\lambda=0
in
M
. Since
\left|\nicefrac{{s\lambda|x}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|=1
,
s\lambda\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}M
and hence
b\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}M
.

(vi)
Suppose
M\models\Lambda_{6}
. Since
\delta\notin\textnormal{Ass}M
,
xb=0
is equivalent to
xs\lambda=0
in
M
. Since
xs=0\geq_{M}c^{\prime}u|x
,

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{xs\lambda=0}}{{c^{\prime}u|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{xs\lambda=0}}{{xs=0}}(M)\right|\cdot\left|\nicefrac{{xs=0}}{{c^{\prime}u|x}}(M)\right|.

By
5.7
,
\left|\nicefrac{{xs\lambda=0}}{{xs=0}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{s|x\wedge x\lambda=0}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|
. So, since
\left|\nicefrac{{x\lambda=0}}{{s|x}}\right|=1
,

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\left(M\right)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{xs\lambda=0}}{{xs=0}}\left(M\right)\right|\cdot\left|\nicefrac{{xs=0}}{{c^{\prime}u|x}}\left(M\right)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x\lambda=0}}{{x=0}}\left(M\right)\right|\cdot\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\left(M\right)\right|.

∎

Proposition 5.10
.

There is an algorithm which, given
w\in W
with
\mathrm{clx}_{2}(w)>1
, outputs
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
such that
\mathrm{clx}_{2}(\underline{w})<\mathrm{clx}_{2}(w)
,
\mathrm{clx}_{1}(\underline{w})\leq\mathrm{clx}_{1}(w)
, and,
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
.

Proof.

We start with a special case. Let
b,c,b^{\prime},c^{\prime}\in R\backslash\{0\}
. Let
P_{6},P^{\prime}_{6},\Lambda_{6},\Lambda^{\prime}_{6}
be as in
5.9
. Let
\Sigma_{1}:=\left|c|x/x=0\right|=1
,
\Sigma_{2}:=\left|b|x/x=0\right|=1
,
\Sigma_{3}:=\left|c^{\prime}|x/x=0\right|=1
,
\Sigma_{4}:=\left|b^{\prime}|x/x=0\right|=1
,
\Sigma_{5}:=\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=1
,
\Sigma_{6}:=\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\right|=1
,
\Sigma_{7}:=\Lambda_{6}
,
\Sigma_{8}:=P_{6}
,
\Sigma_{9}:=\Lambda^{\prime}_{6}
and
\Sigma_{10}:=P^{\prime}_{6}
.

Fix
1\leq i\leq 4
or
7\leq i\leq 10
. Suppose
w
is

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\square E\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}E^{\prime}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi.

Case i=1:
Let
w^{\prime}
be

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\square E\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}E^{\prime}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi.

Then
\mathrm{clx}_{1}w^{\prime}=\mathrm{clx}_{1}w
and
\mathrm{clx}_{2}w^{\prime}<\mathrm{clx}_{2}w
. Since
T_{R}\models w\leftrightarrow w^{\prime}
, we get
w\in V
if and only if
w^{\prime}\in V
.

Case i=2,3,4:
The same argument as for
i=1
works.

Case i=7:
By
5.9
, if
M\models\Sigma_{7}=\Lambda_{6}
then

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}(M)\right|\cdot\left|\nicefrac{{x\lambda=0}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|.

By
5.6
, there is an algorithm which either returns
\Omega:=\bot
, in which case

T_{R}\models\lnot(\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\square E\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}E^{\prime}),

or, a set
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{N}^{2}\times\{=,\geq\}^{2}
such that

\Sigma_{7}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\square E\wedge|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}|\square^{\prime}E^{\prime}

is equivalent, with respect to
T_{R}
, to

\bigvee_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})\in\Omega}\Sigma_{7}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x\lambda=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\square_{1}D_{1}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\right|\square_{2}D_{2}.

If
\Omega:=\{\bot\}
then
w\in V
if and only if
\bot\in\mathbb{V}
and by definition
\mathrm{clx}_{1}\bot\leq\mathrm{clx}_{1}w
and
\mathrm{clx}_{2}\bot<\mathrm{clx}_{2}w
. Otherwise,

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\square E\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}E^{\prime}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{7}\wedge\Xi

is equivalent to

\bigvee_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})\in\Omega}\left|\nicefrac{{x\lambda=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\square_{1}D_{1}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\right|\square_{2}D_{2}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{7}\wedge\Xi.

For each
(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})\in\Omega
, let

w_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})}:=\left|\nicefrac{{x\lambda=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\square_{1}D_{1}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\right|\square_{2}D_{2}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{7}\wedge\Xi.

So
w\in V
if and only if

\bigsqcup_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})\in\Omega}w_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})}\in\mathbb{V}.

For all
(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})\in\Omega
,
\mathrm{clx}_{1}w_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})}=\mathrm{clx}_{1}w
and

\mathrm{clx}_{2}w_{(D_{1},D_{2},\square_{1},\square_{2})}<\mathrm{clx}_{2}w
.

Case i=8,9,10:
The same argument as for
i=7
works.

We now deal with the general case. Let

w:=\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Y}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|\geq g(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\Xi\in W

where
X,Y
are disjoint finite sets of pp-pairs of the form
d|x/x=0
and
xb=0/c|x
,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
and
\Xi
is an auxiliary sentence.

Suppose that
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}},\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\in X\cup Y
are distinct pp-pairs. Let
\Omega\subseteq\Omega_{f,g,10}
such that
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega
if and only if
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\in X_{5}
,
\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\in X_{6}
,
f_{5}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=1
, and
f_{6}(\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}})=1
.

Then
w\in V
if and only if

\bigsqcup_{(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega}\bigsqcap_{i=1}^{10}\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Y}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|\geq g_{i}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi\in\mathbb{V}.

For each
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega
and
1\leq i\leq 10
, let

w_{i,\overline{f},\overline{g}}:=\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Y}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|\geq g_{i}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi.

By definition of
\Omega_{f,g,10}
, for each
1\leq i\leq 10
,
\mathrm{clx}_{1}w_{i,\overline{f},\overline{g}}\leq\mathrm{clx}_{1}w
,
\mathrm{clx}_{2}w_{i,\overline{f},\overline{g}}\leq clx_{2}w
.

By assumption
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\in X
and
f(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})>1
or
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\in Y
and
g(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})>1
. So, since
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\in X_{5}
and
f_{5}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=1
, for each
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,10}
,
\mathrm{clx}_{2}w_{5,\overline{f},\overline{g}}<\mathrm{clx}_{2}w
. Replacing
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}
by
\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}
, the same argument gives
\mathrm{clx}_{2}w_{6,\overline{f},\overline{g}}<\mathrm{clx}_{2}w
.

If
1\leq i\leq 4
or
7\leq i\leq 10
then
w_{i,\overline{f},\overline{g}}
is of the form of the special case considered at the beginning of the proof. Thus we may replace each
w_{i,\overline{f},\overline{g}}
by a lattice combination of
w^{\prime}\in W
such that
\mathrm{clx}_{1}w^{\prime}\leq\mathrm{clx}_{1}w_{i,\overline{f},\overline{g}}
, and, either
\mathrm{clx}_{2}w^{\prime}<\mathrm{clx}_{2}w_{i,\overline{f},\overline{g}}
or
\mathrm{clx}_{2}w^{\prime}\leq\mathrm{clx}_{2}w_{i,\overline{f},\overline{g}}
.
∎

Proof of
5.2
.

By
5.1
, in order to show that
T_{R}
is decidable, it is enough to show that there exists an algorithm which given
w\in W
answers whether
w\in V
or not. By assumption, the set of
w^{\prime}\in V
with
\mathrm{clx}_{1}w^{\prime}\leq 1
and
\mathrm{clx}_{2}w^{\prime}\leq 1
is recursive. Thus the set of
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
with
\mathrm{clx}_{1}\underline{w}\leq 1
and
\mathrm{clx}_{2}\underline{w}\leq 1
is recursive.

Since
\mathbb{N}_{0}
is artinian as an order, iteratively applying
5.8
and
5.10
, gives an algorithm which given
w\in W
with either
\mathrm{clx}_{1}w>1
or
\mathrm{clx}_{2}w>1
outputs
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
such that
\mathrm{clx}_{1}\underline{w},\mathrm{clx}_{2}\underline{w}\leq 1
, and,
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
.
∎

6.
Uniserial modules with finite invariants sentences

Descriptions of the uniserial (and hence indecomposable pure-injective) modules,
U
, over a valuation domain which have
\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}(U)
finite but non-zero for a given pp-pair
\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}
are given for valuation domains with dense value groups in
[
PPT07
]
and for valuation domains with non-dense value groups in
[
Gre15
]
. However, we need a uniform description that works for both valuation domains with dense and non-dense value groups.
This is done in Lemmas
6.1
,
6.2
and
6.3
and used in section
9
and
10
.
The rest of the section is about these modules in preparation for sections
7
,
8
and
10
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
V
be a valuation domain. If
d\in V
and
U
is a uniserial
V
-module such that
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(U)=Ud
is finite and non-zero then
U\cong V/dI
for some
I\lhd V
and
Ud\cong V/I
.

Proof.

For any module
M
,
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{xd=0}}(M)\cong\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(M)
. Thus if
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(U)\cong\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{xd=0}}(U)
is finite but not equal to the zero module then, by
2.12
,
U\cong V/J
for some ideal
J\lhd V
. Since
Ud\neq 0
,
d\notin J
and therefore
dV\supseteq J
. So there exists
I\lhd V
such that
dI=J
.
∎

Note that in the assumptions of the second clause of the next lemma we are not excluding that
I=V
or consequently that
xb=0/c|x(I/bcV)=0
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
V
be a valuation domain and
b,c\in V\backslash\{0\}
. If
U
is a uniserial
V
-module such that
b,c\notin\text{ann}_{V}U
and
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(U)
is finite but non-zero then there exists
I\lhd V
with
b,c\in I
such that
U\equiv I/bcV
and
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(U)\cong V/I
.

Conversely, if
0\neq I\lhd V
is an ideal and
b,c\in I\backslash\{0\}
then
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(I/bcV)\cong V/I
.

Proof.

Let
Q
be the field of fractions of
V
. By
[
Zie84
, pg 168]
, for any non-zero uniserial module
U
, there exist submodules
K\subsetneq J\subseteq Q
such that
U\equiv J/K
. Now
b,c\notin\text{ann}_{V}J/K
imply
(K:b)\subsetneq J
and
cJ\supsetneq K
respectively. Therefore, since
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(J/K)\neq 0
,
xb=0/c|x(J/K)=(K:b)/cJ\cong K/cbJ
. Since
K/cbJ
is a non-zero finite uniserial module, it has the form
V/I
for some proper ideal
I\lhd V
. Therefore
K=\lambda V
for some
\lambda\in Q\backslash\{0\}
and
\lambda^{-1}cbJ=I
. Thus
J/K\cong I/bcV
as required.

Let
0\neq I\lhd V
be an ideal and
b,c\in I\backslash\{0\}
. Then
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(I/bcV)=cV/cI\cong V/I
.
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
V
be a valuation domain and
c\in V
. If
U
is a uniserial
V
-module such that
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(U)
is finite but non-zero then
U\cong V/K
for some ideal
K\lhd R
. Moreover, if
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(U)
is finite but non-zero then either
U\cong V/cI
for some
I\lhd V
and
V/cV\cong\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(U)
, or,
c\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}U
and
U\cong\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(U)
.

Proof.

The first claim is a consequence of
2.12
. If
c\in K
then
c\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}V/K
. If
c\notin K
then
cV\supseteq K
and hence there exists
I\lhd V
such that
K=cI
.
∎

We avoid dealing directly with uniserial
V
-modules
U
such that
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(U)
is finite but non-zero by using duality.

6.1.
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma,\delta)

Definition 6.4
.

Let
r,a,\gamma,\delta\in R
,
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
a prime ideal and
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
an ideal. We write
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma,\delta)
if
rR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
,
a\in I
,
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}
and
\delta\notin I^{\#}
.

The task of this subsection is to show that given an auxiliary sentence
\Xi
and
\lambda\in R\backslash\{0\}
, we can compute
(r_{i},r_{i}a_{i},\gamma_{i},\delta_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq n
such that for any prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideal
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I\models\Xi
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{i},r_{i}a_{i},\gamma_{i},\delta_{i})
for some
1\leq i\leq n
. However, in
10.5
, we will need the
(r_{i},r_{i}a_{i},\gamma_{i},\delta_{i})
that we compute to interact well with the duality defined in
2.3
.

Remark 6.5
.

Let
r,a,\gamma,\delta,\alpha\in R
. For all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma,\delta)
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma\alpha,\delta\alpha)
or
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma(\alpha-1),\delta(\alpha-1))
.

Proof.

This is true because for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
, either
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}
or
\alpha-1\notin\mathfrak{p}
.
∎

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
R
be a Prüfer domain and
b,c,d\in R
with
b\neq 0
. Let
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
be a prime ideal and
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
be an ideal.

(1)

Then
\left|xb=0/c|x(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I)\right|=1
if and only if
b\notin I^{\#}
,
c\notin\mathfrak{p}
,
bcR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
or
1\in I
.

(2)

Then
\left|d|x/x=0(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I)\right|=1
if and only if
d\in I
.

Proof.

(1) For any ideal
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
\left|xb=0/c|x(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I)\right|=1
if and only if
cR_{\mathfrak{p}}+I\supseteq(I:b)
. Since
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a valuation ring,
cR_{\mathfrak{p}}+I\supseteq(I:b)
if and only if
I\supseteq(I:b)
or
cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq(I:b)
. So it is enough to note that
I\supseteq(I:b)
if and only if
b\notin I^{\#}
or
1\in I
, and,
cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq(I:b)
if and only if
bcR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
or
c\notin\mathfrak{p}
.

(2) is obvious.
∎

Lemma 6.7
.

Given
(r,a,\gamma,\delta),(r^{\prime},a^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\in R^{4}
we can compute
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
(r_{i},a_{i},\gamma_{i},\delta_{i})\in R^{4}
for
1\leq i\leq n
such that

(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma,\delta)\text{ and }(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r^{\prime},a^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})

if and only if

(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{i},a_{i},\gamma_{i},\delta_{i})

for some
1\leq i\leq n
, and,

(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\delta,\gamma)\text{ and }(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r^{\prime},a^{\prime},\delta^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})

if and only if

(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{i},a_{i},\delta_{i},\gamma_{i})

for some
1\leq i\leq n
.

Proof.

Let
\alpha,u_{1},u_{2},\beta,v_{1},v_{2}\in R
be such that
r\alpha=r^{\prime}u_{1}
,
r^{\prime}(\alpha-1)=ru_{2}
,
a\beta=a^{\prime}v_{1}
and
a^{\prime}(\beta-1)=av_{2}
. Then
one verifies easily that

(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma,\delta)\text{ and }(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r^{\prime},a^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})

if and only if

(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a^{\prime},\alpha\beta\gamma\gamma^{\prime},\alpha\beta\delta\delta^{\prime})
,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\alpha(\beta-1)\gamma\gamma^{\prime},\alpha(\beta-1)\delta\delta^{\prime})
,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r^{\prime},a^{\prime},(\alpha-1)\beta\gamma\gamma^{\prime},(\alpha-1)\beta\delta\delta^{\prime})
or
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r^{\prime},a,(\alpha-1)(\beta-1)\gamma\gamma^{\prime},(\alpha-1)(\beta-1)\delta\delta^{\prime})
.
∎

Lemma 6.8
.

Given
(r,a,\gamma,\delta)\in R^{4}
and
\lambda\in R\backslash\{0\}
, we can compute
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
(r_{j},a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for
1\leq j\leq n
such that, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

•

(\mathfrak{p},\lambda I)\models(r,a,\gamma,\delta)
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{j},a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq n
and

•

(\mathfrak{p},\lambda I)\models(r,a,\delta,\gamma)
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{j},a_{j},\delta_{j},\gamma_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq n
.

Proof.

Let
\alpha,u,v,\beta,u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\in R
be such that
r\alpha=\lambda u
,
\lambda(\alpha-1)=rv
,
a\beta=\lambda u^{\prime}
and
\lambda(\beta-1)=av^{\prime}
.
By
6.5
,
(\mathfrak{p},\lambda I)\models(r,a,\gamma,\delta)
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},\lambda I)\models(r,a,\alpha\beta\gamma,\alpha\beta\delta)
,
(\mathfrak{p},\lambda I)\models(r,a,\alpha(\beta-1)\gamma,\alpha(\beta-1)\delta)
,
(\mathfrak{p},\lambda I)\models(r,a,(\alpha-1)\beta\gamma,(\alpha-1)\beta\delta)
or
(\mathfrak{p},\lambda I)\models(r,a,(\alpha-1)(\beta-1)\gamma,(\alpha-1)(\beta-1)\delta)
.

It is straightforward to see that:

•

If
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}
then
rR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
if and only if
uR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
.

•

If
\alpha-1\notin\mathfrak{p}
then
rR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
.

•

If
\beta\notin\mathfrak{p}
then
a\in\lambda I
if and only if
u^{\prime}\in I
.

•

If
\beta-1\notin\mathfrak{p}
then
a\in\lambda I
if and only if
1\in I
and
v^{\prime}\notin\mathfrak{p}
(and hence
v^{\prime}\notin I^{\#}
).

Recall that, since
\lambda\neq 0
,
(\lambda I)^{\#}=I^{\#}
. Therefore,
(\mathfrak{p},\lambda I)\models(r,a,\gamma,\delta)
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(u,u^{\prime},\alpha\beta\gamma,\alpha\beta\delta)
,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(u,1,\alpha(\beta-1)v^{\prime}\gamma,\alpha(\beta-1)v^{\prime}\delta)
,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,u^{\prime},(\alpha-1)\beta\gamma,(\alpha-1)\beta\delta)
or
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,1,(\alpha-1)(\beta-1)v^{\prime}\gamma,(\alpha-1)(\beta-1)v^{\prime}\delta)
.
∎

Lemma 6.9
.

Given
(r,a,\gamma,\delta)\in R^{4}
, we can compute
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for
1\leq j\leq n
such that for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

•

(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma,\delta)
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq n
and

•

(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\delta,\gamma)
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\delta_{j},\gamma_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq n
.

Proof.

If
a=0
then
(r,a,\gamma,\delta)=(r,r\cdot 0,\gamma,\delta)
is already of the required form. So suppose
a\neq 0
.

Let
\alpha,u,v\in R
be such that
a\alpha=ru
and
r(\alpha-1)=av
. By
6.5
,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma,\delta)
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma\alpha,\delta\alpha)
or
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma(\alpha-1),\delta(\alpha-1))
. Since
a\alpha=ru
,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma\alpha,\delta\alpha)
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,ru,\gamma\alpha,\delta\alpha)
. Since
r(\alpha-1)=av
,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma(\alpha-1),\delta(\alpha-1))
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(av,a,\gamma(\alpha-1),\delta(\alpha-1))
. Now, since
a\neq 0
,
avR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
and
a\in I
if and only if
v\notin\mathfrak{p}
,
aR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
and
a\in I
. So
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(av,a,\gamma(\alpha-1),\delta(\alpha-1))
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(a,a,\gamma\alpha v,\delta\alpha v)
.

Therefore
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\gamma,\delta)
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,rv,\gamma\alpha,\delta\alpha)
or
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(a,a,\gamma(\alpha-1)v,\delta(\alpha-1)v)
. The same argument shows that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,a,\delta,\gamma)
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,rv,\delta\alpha,\gamma\alpha)
or
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(a,a,\delta(\alpha-1)v,\gamma(\alpha-1)v)
.
∎

Proposition 6.10
.

Given an auxiliary sentence
\Xi
and
\lambda\in R\backslash\{0\}
, we can compute
n\in\mathbb{N}
and for
1\leq j\leq n
,
(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})\in R^{4}
such that, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

•

(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq n
if and only if
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I\models\Xi
, and

•

(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\delta_{j},\gamma_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq n
if and only if
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I\models D\Xi

Proof.

Let
\Xi
be the sentence

\bigwedge_{i=1}^{l^{\prime}}\left|\nicefrac{{d_{i}|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1\wedge\bigwedge_{i=l^{\prime}}^{l}\left|\nicefrac{{xb_{i}=0}}{{c_{i}|x}}\right|=1.

Using
2.15
and
6.6
, we can compute
n_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
for
1\leq i\leq l
and
s_{ij},b_{ij},g_{ij},h_{ij}\in R
for
1\leq i\leq l
and
1\leq j\leq n_{i}
such that for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

•

R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\models\Xi
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models\bigwedge_{i=1}^{l}\bigvee_{j=1}^{n_{i}}(s_{ij},b_{ij},g_{ij},h_{ij})
, and

•

R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\models D\Xi
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models\bigwedge_{i=1}^{l}\bigvee_{j=1}^{n_{i}}(s_{ij},b_{ij},h_{ij},g_{ij})
.

Therefore,
for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

•

R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\models\Xi
if and only if

(\mathfrak{p},I)\models\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma:\{1,\ldots,l\}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}\\
\sigma(i)\leq n_{i}\end{subarray}}\ \bigwedge_{i=1}^{l}(s_{i\sigma(i)},b_{i\sigma(i)},g_{i\sigma(i)},h_{i\sigma(i)}),\text{ and}

•

R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\models D\Xi
if and only if

(\mathfrak{p},I)\models\bigvee_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma:\{1,\ldots,l\}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}\\
\sigma(i)\leq n_{i}\end{subarray}}\ \bigwedge_{i=1}^{l}(s_{i\sigma(i)},b_{i\sigma(i)},h_{i\sigma(i)},g_{i\sigma(i)}).

We can use
6.7
, to replace the conjunction
\bigwedge_{i=1}^{l}(s_{i\sigma(i)},b_{i\sigma(i)},g_{i\sigma(i)},h_{i\sigma(i)})
, for each
\sigma
, by a disjunction to produce and
(s^{\prime}_{\sigma k},b^{\prime}_{\sigma k},g^{\prime}_{\sigma k},\delta^{\prime}_{\sigma k})
for
1\leq k\leq m_{\sigma}
such that

•

R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\models\Xi
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(s^{\prime}_{\sigma k},b^{\prime}_{\sigma k},g^{\prime}_{\sigma k},h^{\prime}_{\sigma k})
for some
1\leq k\leq m_{\sigma}
, and

•

R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\models D\Xi
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(s^{\prime}_{\sigma k},b^{\prime}_{\sigma k},h^{\prime}_{\sigma k},g^{\prime}_{\sigma k})
for some
1\leq k\leq m_{\sigma}
.

Applying
6.8
to each
(s^{\prime}_{\sigma k},b^{\prime}_{\sigma k},h^{\prime}_{\sigma k},g^{\prime}_{\sigma k})
, we compute
(r^{\prime}_{j},a^{\prime}_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j},\delta^{\prime}_{j})
for
1\leq j\leq m
such that

•

R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I\models\Xi
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r^{\prime}_{j},a^{\prime}_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j},\delta^{\prime}_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq m
, and

•

R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I\models D\Xi
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r^{\prime}_{j},a^{\prime}_{j},\delta^{\prime}_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq m
.

Finally, applying
6.9
to each
(r^{\prime}_{j},a^{\prime}_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j},\delta^{\prime}_{j})
, we can compute
(r_{i},r_{i}a_{i},\gamma_{i},\delta_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq n
such that

•

R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I\models\Xi
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{i},r_{i}a_{i},\gamma_{i},\delta_{i})
for some
1\leq i\leq n
, and

•

R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I\models D\Xi
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{i},r_{i}a_{i},\delta_{i},\gamma_{i})
for some
1\leq i\leq n
. ∎

6.2.
Simplification of
\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)
and
\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})

The results of this subsection will be used in sections
7
and
8
.
In this subsection, we no longer need to worry about stability under duality.

Remark 6.11
.

Let
a,b\in R
and
\alpha,u,v\in R
be such that
a\alpha=bu
and
b(\alpha-1)=av
. For prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

•

a\in bI
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,u,\alpha,1)
or
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,1,v(\alpha-1),1)
, and,

•

aR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq bI
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(u,0,\alpha,1)
or
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,0,\alpha-1,1)
.

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain and
\lambda\in R\backslash\{0\}
.

(1)

Given
d\in R
, we can compute finite sets
S_{1},S_{2},S_{3}\subseteq R^{4}
,
\rho:\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}S_{i}\rightarrow\{1,2,3\}
and
s:\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}S_{i}\rightarrow R
such that for all
q\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}S_{i}
,
q\in S_{\rho(q)}
, and, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, there exists
q\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}S_{i}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
, and

\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/s(q)I\right|,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=1$;}\\
\left|s(q)R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\right|,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=2$;}\\
1,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=3$.}\end{array}\right.

Furthermore, if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
for some
q\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{3}S_{i}
and
\rho(q)=2
then
s(q)R_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
.

(2)

Given
b,c\in R
, we can compute finite sets
S_{1},\ldots,S_{5}\subseteq R^{4}
,
\rho:\bigcup_{i=1}^{5}S_{i}\rightarrow\{1,\ldots,5\}
and
s:\bigcup_{i=1}^{5}S_{i}\rightarrow R
such that for all
q\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{5}S_{i}
,
q\in S_{\rho(q)}
, and, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, there exists
q\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{5}S_{i}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
, and

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I\right|,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=1$;}\\
\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=2$;}\\
\left|I/bI\right|,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=3$;}\\
1,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=4$;}\\
\left|I/s(q)R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=5$.}\end{array}\right.

Furthermore, if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
for some
q\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{5}S_{i}
and
\rho(q)=5
then
s(q)\in I
. Moreover, if
b=0
then we may assume that
S_{3}=S_{4}=S_{5}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

(a) If
d=0
then
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(N)=0
for all
R
-modules
N
. So set
S_{1}=S_{2}=\emptyset
and
S_{3}:=\{(1,0,1,1)\}
.

Suppose
d\neq 0
. Let
\alpha,u,v\in R
be such that
d\alpha=\lambda u
and
\lambda(\alpha-1)=dv
.

•

If
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q_{1}:=(1,0,\alpha-1,1)
then

\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|dR_{\mathfrak{p}}/dvI\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/vI\right|.

•

If
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q_{2}:=(u,0,\alpha,1)
then by definition
uR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
, and,

\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|\lambda uR_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I\right|=\left|uR_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\right|.

•

If
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q_{3}:=(1,u,\alpha,1)
then
d\in\lambda I
and hence
\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=1
.

For all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
, either
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}
or
\alpha-1\notin\mathfrak{p}
, and, for all ideal
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, either
uR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
or
u\in I
. Therefore for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, either
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q_{1}
,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q_{2}
or
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q_{3}
. So, set
S_{i}:=\{q_{i}\}
for
1\leq i\leq 3
, and, set
s(q_{1}):=v
,
s(q_{2}):=u
and
s(q_{3}):=1
.

(b)
For all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|\frac{(\lambda I:b)+cR_{\mathfrak{p}}}{cR_{\mathfrak{p}}+\lambda I}\right|.

First suppose
b=0
. If
c\in\lambda I
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I\right|
, and, if
cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|
. We can use
6.11
to compute finite sets
S_{1},S_{2}\subseteq R^{4}
such that for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, there exists
q\in S_{1}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
if and only if
c\in\lambda I
, and, there exists
q\in S_{2}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
if and only if
cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
. Set
S_{3}=S_{4}=S_{5}=\emptyset
.

Now suppose
b\neq 0
. If
bR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
then

\left|\frac{(\lambda I:b)+cR_{\mathfrak{p}}}{cR_{\mathfrak{p}}+\lambda I}\right|=\left|\frac{\lambda I+bcR_{\mathfrak{p}}}{bcR_{\mathfrak{p}}+b\lambda I}\right|.

So

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I\right|,&\hbox{if $b,c\in\lambda I$;}\\
\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|,&\hbox{if $b\in\lambda I$ and $cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I$;}\\
\left|I/bI\right|,&\hbox{if $bR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I$ and $c\in\lambda I$;}\\
1,&\hbox{if $bcR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I$;}\\
\left|\lambda I/bcR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|,&\hbox{if $bR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I$, $cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I$ and $bc\in\lambda I$.}\end{array}\right.

Therefore it is enough to compute:

•

S_{1}
such that
b,c\in\lambda I
if and only if there exists
q\in S_{1}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
,

•

S_{2}
such that
b\in\lambda I
and
cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
if and only if exists
q\in S_{2}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
,

•

S_{3}
such that
bR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
and
c\in\lambda I
and if and only if exists
q\in S_{3}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
,

•

S_{4}
such that
bcR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
if and only if there exists
q\in S_{4}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
, and,

•

S_{5}
and for each
q\in S_{5}
,
s_{q}\in R
such that
bR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
,
cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
and
bc\in\lambda I
if and only if there exists
q\in S_{5}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
and such that, in this situation
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|I/s_{q}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|
.

It is easy to compute
S_{1},\ldots,S_{4}
using
6.11
and
6.7
.

Let
\epsilon,r,s
be such that
bc\epsilon=\lambda s
and
\lambda(\epsilon-1)=bcr
. By
6.11
,
bc\in\lambda I
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,s,\epsilon,1)
or
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,1,r(\epsilon-1),1)
. If
bR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
,
cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
and
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,s,\epsilon,1)
then

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|\lambda I/bcR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|=\left|I/sR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|.

Use
6.11
and
6.7
to compute
S_{5}^{\prime}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
for some
q\in S_{5}^{\prime}
if and only if
bR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
,
cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
and
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,s,\epsilon,1)
.

If
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,1,r(\epsilon-1),1)
then
I=R_{\mathfrak{p}}
and

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|\lambda I/bcR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|=\left|I/1R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|.

Let
S_{5}^{\prime\prime}:=\{(1,1,r(\epsilon-1),1)\}
and let
S_{5}:=S_{5}^{\prime}\cup S_{5}^{\prime\prime}
. Set
s(q):=s
if
s\in S_{5}^{\prime}
and
s(q):=1
otherwise. Note that in both cases, by definition,
s(q)\in I
.
∎

Proposition 6.13
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain,
\lambda\in R\backslash\{0\}
and
Z
a finite subset of pp-pairs of the form
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}
and
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}
. Let

T_{Z}:=\{\mu:Z\rightarrow\{1,\ldots,5\}\ |\ \text{ for all }\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\in Z\ \ \mu(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})\leq 3\}.

We can compute
S_{Z}
a finite subset of
R^{4}
,
\rho_{Z}:S_{Z}\rightarrow T_{Z}
and
s_{Z}:S_{Z}\times Z\rightarrow R
such that for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,

(1)

there exists
q\in S_{Z}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
,

(2)

(a)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=1
then
\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/s_{Z}(q,\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})I\right|

(b)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=2
then
\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|s_{Z}(q,\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\right|

(c)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=3
then
\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=1

(3)

(a)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=1
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I\right|

(b)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=2
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|

(c)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=3
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|I/bI\right|

(d)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=4
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=1

(e)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=5
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|I/s_{Z}(q,\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|

Furthermore, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
and
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=2
then
s_{Z}(q,\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})R_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
, and, if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
and
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=5
then
s_{Z}(q,\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})\in I
. Moreover, if
b=0
then we may assume
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})\in\{1,2\}
for all
q\in S_{Z}
.

Proof.

We prove the proposition iteratively. Let
\lambda\in R\backslash\{0\}
and
Z
be a finite set of pp-pairs of the form
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}
or
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}
. If
|Z|=1
then
6.12
gives the required result. Suppose that
S_{Z},\rho_{Z}
and
s_{Z}
are as in the statement. We construct
S_{Z^{\prime}},\rho_{Z^{\prime}}
and
s_{Z^{\prime}}
for
Z^{\prime}=Z\cup\{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\}
.

Suppose that
\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}
is either of the form
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}
or
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}
and
\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\notin Z
. Let
S_{1},\ldots,S_{5}
,
s:\bigcup_{i=1}^{5}S_{i}\rightarrow R
and
\rho:\bigcup_{i=1}^{5}S_{i}\rightarrow\{1,\ldots,5\}
be as in
6.12
(1) or (2), as appropriate (if
\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}
is of the form
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}
then set
S_{4}=S_{5}=\emptyset
). By
6.7
, for each
q\in S_{Z}
and
p\in S_{i}
, we can compute a finite set
S_{q,p,i}\subseteq R^{4}
such that for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
and
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models p
if and only if there exists
q^{\prime}\in S_{q,p,i}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q^{\prime}
. Let
S_{q,i}:=\bigcup_{p\in S_{i}}S_{q,p,i}
, let
S_{q}:=\bigcup_{i=1}^{5}S_{q,i}
and let
S_{Z^{\prime}}:=\bigcup_{q\in S_{Z}}S_{q}
.

For all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, by assumption, there exists
q\in S_{Z}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
and, by
6.12
, there exists
1\leq i\leq 5
, such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q^{\prime}
for some
q^{\prime}\in S_{i}
. Therefore,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q^{\prime\prime}
for some
q^{\prime\prime}\in S_{q,i}\subseteq S_{Z^{\prime}}
. So (a) holds for
S_{Z^{\prime}}
.

Define
\rho_{Z^{\prime}}:S_{Z^{\prime}}\rightarrow T_{Z^{\prime}}
by setting

\rho_{Z^{\prime}}(q^{\prime})(\nicefrac{{\sigma}}{{\tau}}):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\min\{\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{\sigma}}{{\tau}})\ |\ q^{\prime}\in S_{q}\},&\hbox{if $\nicefrac{{\sigma}}{{\tau}}\in Z$;}\\
\min\{1\leq i\leq 5\ |\ q^{\prime}\in S_{q,i}\text{ for some }q\in S_{Z}\},&\hbox{ if $\nicefrac{{\sigma}}{{\tau}}=\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}$.}\end{array}\right.

For
\sigma/\tau\in Z
, set
s_{Z^{\prime}}(q^{\prime},\nicefrac{{\sigma}}{{\tau}})
to be
s_{Z}(q,\sigma/\tau)
for some
q\in S_{Z}
where
q^{\prime}\in S_{q}
and
\rho_{Z^{\prime}}(q^{\prime})(\nicefrac{{\sigma}}{{\tau}})=\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{\sigma}}{{\tau}})
. For
q^{\prime}\in S_{Z^{\prime}}
, set
s_{Z^{\prime}}(q^{\prime},\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})
to be
s(p)
for some
p\in S_{j}
where
j=\rho_{Z^{\prime}}(q^{\prime})(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})
and
q^{\prime}\in S_{q,p,j}
.

Now, for
\nicefrac{{\sigma}}{{\tau}}\in Z
, properties (b) or (c), as appropriate, are inherited from those properties holding for
\rho_{Z}
and
s_{Z}
and for
\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}
, properties (b) or (c), as appropriate, are inherited from
\rho
and
s
.
∎

Lemma 6.14
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain and
\lambda\in R\backslash\{0\}
.

(1)

Given
b,c\in R
, we can compute finite sets
S_{1},\ldots,S_{6}\subseteq R^{4}
,
\rho:\bigcup_{i=1}^{6}S_{i}\rightarrow\{1,\ldots,6\}
and
s:\bigcup_{i=1}^{5}S_{i}\rightarrow R
such that for all
q\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{6}S_{i}
,
q\in S_{\rho(q)}
, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
\lambda\in I
, there exists
q\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{6}S_{i}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
, and,

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left|I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=1$;}\\
\left|I/cI\right|,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=2$;}\\
\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/bR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=3$;}\\
1,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=4$;}\\
\left|s(q)R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\right|,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=5$;}\\
\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/s(q)I\right|,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=6$.}\end{array}\right.

Furthermore, if
\rho(q)=5
and
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
then
s(q)R_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
.

(2)

Given
d\in R
, we can compute finite sets
S_{1},S_{2}\subseteq R^{4}
,
\rho:\bigcup_{i=1}^{2}S_{i}\rightarrow\{1,2\}
and
s:\bigcup_{i=1}^{2}S_{i}\rightarrow R
such that for all
q\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{2}S_{i}
,
q\in S_{\rho(q)}
, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
\lambda\in I
, there exists
q\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{2}S_{i}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
, and,

\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left|I/s(q)R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=1$;}\\
0,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q$ and $\rho(q)=2$.}\end{array}\right.

Furthermore, if
\rho(q)=1
and
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
then
s(q)\in I
.

Proof.

(a)Let
\alpha,r,s\in R
be such that
\lambda\alpha=bcr
and
bc(\alpha-1)=\lambda s
.

Claim:
If
\lambda\in I
then

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left|I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|,&\hbox{if (1) $bI\subseteq\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}$ and $cI\subseteq\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}$;}\\
\left|I/cI\right|,&\hbox{if (2) $bI\subseteq\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}$ and $\lambda\in cI$;}\\
\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/bR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|,&\hbox{if (3) $b\in\lambda I$ and $cI\subseteq\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}$;}\\
1,&\hbox{if (4) $\lambda\in bI$, $\lambda\in cI$, $r\in I$ and $\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}$;}\\
\left|rR_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\right|,&\hbox{if (5) $\lambda\in bI$, $\lambda\in cI$, $rR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I$ and $\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}$;}\\
\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/sI\right|,&\hbox{if (6) $\lambda\in bI$, $\lambda\in cI$ and $\alpha-1\notin\mathfrak{p}$.}\end{array}\right.

Note that

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|\frac{(\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}:b)\cap I+cI}{cI+\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}}\right|.

For all
a\in R
,
a\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}
if and only if
aI\subseteq\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Therefore the equalities for conditions
(1)
and
(2)
hold. If
\lambda\in bI\subseteq bR_{\mathfrak{p}}
then
b\neq 0
since
\lambda\neq 0
and
(\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}:b)\cap I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}\cong R_{\mathfrak{p}}/bR_{\mathfrak{p}}
. So the equality for condition
(3)
holds.

When proving the equalities for
(4)
,
(5)
and
(6)
, we may assume
b\neq 0
and
c\neq 0
since
\lambda\neq 0
,
\lambda\in cI
and
\lambda\in bI
. Moreover,

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|(\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}:b)+cI/cI\right|=\left|\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}+bcI/bcI\right|.

If
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}
and
r\in I
then
\lambda\in bcI
. So the equality for condition
(4)
holds.

Suppose condition (5) holds. Then
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}
implies
\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}=bcrR_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Since
rR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
,
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda I)\right|=\left|rR_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\right|
. So the equality for condition (5) holds.

Suppose condition (6) holds. Since
\alpha-1\notin\mathfrak{p}
,
bcI=\lambda sI
. So
\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}+bcI/bcI\cong R_{\mathfrak{p}}/sI
. So the equality for condition (6) holds. So we have proved the claim.

Given a finite set of conditions of the form
\beta\notin\mathfrak{p}
,
a\in\lambda I
or
aR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq\lambda I
, using
6.7
and
6.11
, we can compute a finite set
S\subseteq R^{4}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)
satisfies these conditions if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
for some
q\in S
. So for each conditions (
i
) for
1\leq i\leq 6
in the claim, we can compute
S_{i}\subseteq R^{4}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)
satisfies
(i)
and
\lambda\in I
if and only if there exists
q\in S_{i}
with
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
. Moreover, it is easy to see that, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, there exists
1\leq i\leq 6
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)
satisfies
(i)
. Let
\rho:\bigcup_{i=1}^{6}S_{i}\rightarrow\{1,\ldots,6\}
be such that
q\in S_{\rho(q)}
. Finally, set
s(q)=1
if
\rho(q)\leq 4
,
s(q)=r
if
\rho(q)=5
and
s(q)=s
if
\rho(q)=6
.

(b) The case
d=0
is done as in
6.12
. Suppose
d\neq 0
. Let
\alpha,r,s\in R
be such that
d\alpha=\lambda r
and
\lambda(\alpha-1)=ds
. If either
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}
, or,
\alpha-1\notin\mathfrak{p}
and
sR_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
then
dI\subseteq\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}
. If
\alpha-1\notin\mathfrak{p}
and
s\in I
then
dsR_{\mathfrak{p}}=\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}\subseteq dI
. Since
d\neq 0
,
dI/dsR_{\mathfrak{p}}\cong I/sR_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Therefore

\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|\frac{dI+\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}}\right|=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,0,\alpha,1)$;}\\
1,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(s,0,\alpha-1,1)$;}\\
\left|I/sR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|,&\hbox{if $(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,s,\alpha-1,1)$.}\end{array}\right.

It is now clear how to define,
S_{1},S_{2}
,
\rho
and
s
.
∎

Proposition 6.15
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain,
\lambda\in R\backslash\{0\}
and
Z
a finite subset of pp-pairs of the form
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}
and
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}
. Let

T_{Z}:=\{\mu:Z\rightarrow\{1,\ldots,6\}\ |\ \mu(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})\leq 2\}.

We can compute
S_{Z}
a finite subset of
R^{4}
,
\rho_{Z}:S_{Z}\rightarrow T_{Z}
and
s_{Z}:S_{Z}\times Z\rightarrow R
such that, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
\lambda\in I
, if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
for some
q\in S_{Z}
then
\lambda\in I
, and, if
\lambda\in I
then

(1)

there exists
q\in S_{Z}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
,

(2)

(a)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=1
then
\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|I/s_{Z}(q,\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})R_{\mathfrak{m}}\right|

(b)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=2
then
\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=1

(3)

(a)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=1
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|

(b)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=2
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|I/cI\right|

(c)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=3
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/bR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|

(d)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=4
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=1

(e)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=5
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|s(q,\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\right|

(f)

if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=6
then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(I/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/s(q,\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})I\right|

Furthermore, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=1
and
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
then
s_{Z}(q,\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})\in I
, and, if
\rho_{Z}(q)(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=5
and
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
then
s_{Z}(q,\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})R_{\mathfrak{p}}\supseteq I
.

Proof.

This can be proved similarly to
6.13
by replacing
6.12
by
6.14
. The extra condition that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q
for some
q\in S_{Z}
implies
\lambda\in I
can be incorporated using
6.7
, since
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,\lambda,1,1)
if and only if
\lambda\in I
.
∎

7.
Finite modules

In this section we investigate the consequences of
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
being recursive, and of

\mathrm{EPP}(R)
and the radical relation being recursive. In particular, we show that for a recursive Prüfer domain the theory of
R
-modules of size
n
is decidable
uniformly in
n
if and only if
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
is recursive,
7.6
.

Observe that finite modules over a Prüfer domain
R
are finite direct sums of modules of the form
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I
where
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
is a prime ideal and
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is an ideal. There are many ways of seeing this. If
M
is finite then
M
is pure-injective. So, by
2.2
and
2.10
, there exist prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and uniserial
R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
-modules
U_{i}
such that
M
is elementary equivalent, and hence isomorphic, to
\oplus_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}
. The desired result now follows from
2.12
.

Let
W
be the set of tuples
(f,g,\overline{a},\gamma)
where

(1)

f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
where
X:=X_{0}\cup\{\star\}
,
X_{0}
is a finite subset of
R
and
\star\notin R
,

(2)

g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
where
Y
is a finite subset of
R
,

(3)

\overline{a}:=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m})
is a finite tuple of elements of
R
and
\gamma\in R
.

Let
V
be the set of
(f,g,\overline{a},\gamma)\in W
such that, for some
h\in\mathbb{N}
and for
1\leq i\leq h
, there exist a prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
and an ideal
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
such that
a_{j}\in I_{i}
for
1\leq j\leq m
and
1\leq i\leq h
,
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
,

|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/I_{i}|=f(\star),

|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/eR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}|=f(e)\ \ \text{ \ for \ }e\in X_{0}

and

|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/eR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}|\geq g(e)\ \ \text{ \ for \ }e\in Y.

We write
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})_{1\leq i\leq h}\models(f,g,\overline{a},\gamma)
. By convention,
\emptyset\models(f,g,\overline{a},\gamma)
if
f(e)=1
for all
e\in X
and
g(e)=1
for all
e\in Y
and in this situation,
(f,g,\overline{a},\gamma)\in V
.

As in
4.2
,
\mathbb{W}
will denote bounded distributive lattice generated by
W
and
\mathbb{V}
will denote the (prime) filter in
\mathbb{W}
generated by
V
.

Let
W_{0}
be the subset of elements of the form
(f,\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma)
with
|X_{0}|\leq 1
and let
W_{1}
be the subset of elements of the form
(f,\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma)
where, in both cases,
\emptyset
denotes the function from the empty set to
\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathbb{W}_{0}
, respectively
\mathbb{W}_{1}
, denote the lattice generated by
W_{0}\cup\{\top,\bot\}
in
\mathbb{W}
, respectively the lattice generated by
W_{1}\cup\{\top,\bot\}
in
\mathbb{W}
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain. There is an algorithm which given
w\in W_{1}
returns
\underline{u}\in\mathbb{W}_{0}
, such that
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{u}\in\mathbb{V}
.

Proof.

Define
\text{clx}(f,\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma):=(|X_{0}|,\prod_{e\in X_{0}}f(e))
and order
\mathbb{N}_{0}\times\mathbb{N}
lexicographically. For any
w\in W_{1}
with
|X_{0}|>1
, we show how to compute
\underline{u}\in\mathbb{W}_{1}
, such that
\mathrm{clx}\underline{u}<\mathrm{clx}w
and
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{u}\in\mathbb{V}
. Since the lexicographic order on
\mathbb{N}_{0}\times\mathbb{N}
is artinian, this is sufficient to prove the lemma.

Take
e_{1},e_{2}\in X_{0}
non-equal. Let
\alpha,r,s\in R
be such that
e_{1}\alpha=e_{2}r
and
e_{2}(\alpha-1)=e_{1}s
. Let
\Omega
be the set of pairs of functions
(f_{1},f_{2})
such that
f_{1}:X\cup\{r\}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
,
f_{2}:X\cup\{s\}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
and

(1)

f_{1}(e)f_{2}(e)=f(e)
for all
e\in X
,

(2)

f_{1}(e_{1})=f_{1}(e_{2})f(r)
, and

(3)

f_{2}(e_{2})=f_{2}(e_{1})f(s)
.

Let
\underline{u}
be

\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(f_{1},f_{2})\in\Omega\\
f_{1}(e_{2})\neq 1,f_{2}(e_{1})\neq 1\end{subarray}}(f_{1}|_{(X\cup\{r\})\backslash\{e_{1}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma\alpha)\sqcap(f_{2}|_{(X\cup\{s\})\backslash\{e_{2}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma(\alpha-1))

\sqcup\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(f_{1},f_{2})\in\Omega\\
f_{1}(e_{2})=1,f_{2}(e_{1})\neq 1\end{subarray}}(f_{1}|_{X\backslash\{e_{2}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},e_{2}\gamma\alpha)\sqcap(f_{2}|_{(X\cup\{s\})\backslash\{e_{2}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma(\alpha-1))

\sqcup\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(f_{1},f_{2})\in\Omega\\
f_{1}(e_{2})\neq 1,f_{2}(e_{1})=1\end{subarray}}(f_{1}|_{(X\cup\{r\})\backslash\{e_{1}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma\alpha)\sqcap(f_{2}|_{X\backslash\{e_{1}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},e_{1}\gamma(\alpha-1))

\sqcup\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(f_{1},f_{2})\in\Omega\\
f_{1}(e_{2})=1,f_{2}(e_{1})=1\end{subarray}}(f_{1}|_{X\backslash\{e_{2}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},e_{2}\gamma\alpha)\sqcap(f_{2}|_{X\backslash\{e_{1}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},e_{1}\gamma(\alpha-1)).

Claim:

w\in V
if and only if
\underline{u}\in\mathbb{V}

For all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
,
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}
implies
e_{1}R_{\mathfrak{p}}=e_{1}\alpha R_{\mathfrak{p}}=e_{2}rR_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\alpha-1\notin\mathfrak{p}
implies
e_{2}R_{\mathfrak{p}}=e_{2}(\alpha-1)R_{\mathfrak{p}}=e_{1}sR_{\mathfrak{p}}
. So,
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}
implies

\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/e_{1}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/e_{2}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|\cdot\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/rR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|

and

\alpha-1\notin\mathfrak{p}
implies

\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/e_{2}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/e_{1}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|\cdot\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/sR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
is a prime ideal and
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
is an ideal for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})_{1\leq i\leq h}\models(f,\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma)
. By reordering, we may assume that
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq h^{\prime}
and
\alpha-1\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
for
h^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq h
. Let
f_{1}(\star):=|\oplus_{i=1}^{h^{\prime}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/I_{i}|
and
f_{2}(\star):=|\oplus_{i=h^{\prime}+1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/I_{i}|
. For each
e\in X_{0}\cup\{r\}
, let
f_{1}(e)=|\oplus_{i=1}^{h^{\prime}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/eR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}|
and for each
e\in X_{0}\cup\{s\}
, let
f_{2}(e)=|\oplus_{i=h^{\prime}+1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/eR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}|
. It follows from the first paragraph of the proof of this claim that
(f_{1},f_{2})\in\Omega
.

If
f_{1}(e_{2})\neq 1
then

(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})_{1\leq i\leq h^{\prime}}\models(f_{1}|_{(X\cup\{r\})\backslash\{e_{1}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma\alpha)

and if
f_{2}(e_{1})\neq 1
then

(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})_{h^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq h}\models(f_{2}|_{(X\cup\{s\})\backslash\{e_{2}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma(\alpha-1)).

By definition, if
f_{1}(e_{2})=1
, then
e_{2}\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq h^{\prime}
and if
f_{2}(e_{1})=1
then
e_{1}\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
for
h^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq h
. So if
f_{1}(e_{2})=1
then

(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})_{1\leq i\leq h^{\prime}}\models(f_{1}|_{X\backslash\{e_{2}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},e_{2}\gamma\alpha)

and if
f_{2}(e_{1})=1
then

(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})_{h^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq h}\models(f_{2}|_{X\backslash\{e_{1}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},e_{1}\gamma(\alpha-1)).

So we have shown that if
w\in V
then one of the components of the join defining
\underline{u}
is in
\mathbb{V}
and hence
\underline{u}\in\mathbb{V}
.

Conversely, take
(f_{1},f_{2})\in\Omega
. Suppose
f_{1}(e_{2})\neq 1
and

(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})_{1\leq i\leq h^{\prime}}\models(f_{1}|_{(X\cup\{r\})\backslash\{e_{1}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma\alpha).

Since
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq h^{\prime}
,

\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h^{\prime}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/e_{1}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h^{\prime}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/e_{2}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|\cdot\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h^{\prime}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/rR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=f_{1}(e_{2})\cdot f_{1}(r)=f_{1}(e_{1})

by definition of
\Omega
. So

(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})_{1\leq i\leq h^{\prime}}\models(f_{1},\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma)
.

Suppose that
f_{2}(e_{1})=1
and

(\mathfrak{q}_{i},J_{i})_{1\leq i\leq h^{\prime\prime}}\models(f_{2}|_{X\backslash\{e_{1}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},e_{1}\gamma(\alpha-1)).

Then

(\mathfrak{q}_{i},J_{i})_{1\leq i\leq h^{\prime\prime}}\models(f_{2},\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma)

because
e_{1}\notin\mathfrak{q}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq h^{\prime\prime}
implies

\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h^{\prime\prime}}R_{\mathfrak{q}_{i}}/e_{1}R_{\mathfrak{q}_{i}}\right|=1=f_{1}(e_{1}).

So, setting
\mathfrak{p}_{i}:=\mathfrak{q}_{i-h^{\prime}}
and
I_{i}:=J_{i-h^{\prime}}
for
h^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq h^{\prime}+h^{\prime\prime}=h
,

(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})_{1\leq i\leq h}\models(f,\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma)

because
f_{1}(e)f_{2}(e)=f(e)
for all
e\in X
.
We leave the case
f_{1}(e_{2})\neq 1
and
f_{2}(e_{1})\neq 1
, the case
f_{1}(e_{2})=1
and
f_{2}(e_{1})\neq 1
and the case
f_{1}(e_{2})=f_{2}(e_{2})=1
to the reader.

Claim:

\mathrm{clx}\underline{u}<\mathrm{clx}w

We show that each of the components,
u^{\prime}
, of the lattice combination defining
\underline{u}
have
\mathrm{clx}u^{\prime}<\mathrm{clx}w
. We only consider the components involving
f_{1}
; the result for those involving
f_{2}
follows similarly.

If
f_{1}(e_{2})=1
then
\mathrm{clx}(f_{1}|_{X\backslash\{e_{2}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},e_{2}\gamma\alpha)<\mathrm{clx}w
since
|X_{0}|>|X_{0}\backslash\{e_{2}\}|
. If
f_{1}(e_{2})>1
then

\frac{f_{1}(r)}{f(e_{1})}=\frac{f_{1}(e_{2})f_{1}(r)}{f_{1}(e_{2})f(e_{1})}=\frac{f_{1}(e_{1})}{f_{1}(e_{2})f(e_{1})}<1

since
f_{1}(e_{1})/f(e_{1})\leq 1
.
So

f_{1}(r)\cdot\prod_{x\in X_{0}\backslash\{e_{1}\}}f_{1}(x)\leq f_{1}(r)\cdot\prod_{x\in X_{0}\backslash\{e_{1}\}}f(x)=\frac{f_{1}(r)}{f(e_{1})}\cdot\prod_{x\in X_{0}}f(x)<\prod_{x\in X_{0}}f(x).

Therefore
\text{clx}(f_{1}|_{(X\cup\{r\})\backslash\{e_{1}\}},\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma\alpha)<\text{clx}w
.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain. If
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
is recursive then there is an algorithm which given
w\in W_{1}
answers whether
w\in V
or not.

Proof.

Let
w=(f,\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma)
and suppose that
X_{0}=\{e\}
i.e.
w\in W_{0}
. Let
P
be the set of prime divisors of
f(\star)\cdot f(e)
. If
P
is empty then
f(\star)=f(e)=1
and
w\in V
. Otherwise, for each
p\in P
, let
n_{p}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
m_{p}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
be such that
f(\star)=\prod_{p\in P}p^{n_{p}}
and
f(e)=\prod_{p\in P}p^{m_{p}}
. For any prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
,
e\in R
and ideal
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, if
|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/eR_{\mathfrak{p}}|
(respectively
|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I|
) is finite then it is a power of
|R/\mathfrak{p}|
. Thus a prime power. Hence
w\in V
if and only if
(p,n_{p};\overline{a};\gamma;e,m_{p})\in\mathrm{EPP}_{*}(R)
for all
p\in P
.

If
w\in W_{1}
then by
7.1
, we can compute
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}_{0}
such that
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
. Therefore, by the previous paragraph there is an algorithm which, given
w\in W_{1}
answers whether
w\in V
or not.
∎

Corollary 7.3
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain with
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
recursive. There is an algorithm which given
r,a,\gamma,\delta\in R
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
e_{j}\in R
for
1\leq j\leq n
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
N_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
for
1\leq j\leq n
, answers whether there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that

(1)

(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})\models(r,a,\gamma,\delta)
for
1\leq i\leq h
,

(2)

\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/I_{i}\right|=N
, and

(3)

\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/e_{j}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=N_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq n
.
