Proof.

Applying
6.9
, we may reduce to the case where
a=ra^{\prime}
. We may also assume
r\neq 0
since
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(0,b^{\prime},\gamma,\delta)
implies
I=0
and hence
\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|
which is infinite.

For any prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideal
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,ra^{\prime},\gamma,\delta)
if and only if there exists
J\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
such that
I=rJ
and
(\mathfrak{p},J)\models(1,a^{\prime},\gamma,\delta)
. Note that, because
R
is a domain,
\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/rJ\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/J\right|\cdot\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/rR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|
.

Therefore, there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
satisfying
(1),(2)
and
(3)
if and only if there exist
N^{\prime},N^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
with
N^{\prime}\cdot N^{\prime\prime}=N
,
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
J_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that

(1)

(\mathfrak{p}_{i},J_{i})\models(1,a^{\prime},\gamma,\delta)
for
1\leq i\leq h
,

(2)

\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/J_{i}\right|=N^{\prime}
, and

(3)

\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/rR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=N^{\prime\prime}
,
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/e_{j}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=N_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq n
.

By
7.2
, there is an algorithm which answers whether there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
J_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
satisfying (a),(b) and (c).
∎

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain such that
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
and the radical relation are recursive. There is an algorithm which, given
(f,g,\overline{a},\gamma)\in W
, answers whether there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})_{1\leq i\leq h}\models(f,g,\overline{a},\gamma)
.

Proof.

Suppose
(f,g,\overline{a},\gamma)\in W
.

Case
Y=\emptyset
:
This is
7.2
.

Case
|Y|=1
:
Suppose that
X_{0}:=\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\}
and
Y:=\{e\}
.

If
\gamma\prod_{j=1}^{n}e_{j}\notin\textnormal{rad}eR
then there exists a prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}
such that
e_{j}\notin\mathfrak{p}
for
1\leq j\leq n
,
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}
and
e\in\mathfrak{p}
. Then
|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/e_{j}R_{\mathfrak{p}}|=1
for
1\leq j\leq n
and
|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/eR_{\mathfrak{p}}|>1
. So
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})_{i=1}^{h}\models(f,\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma)
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})_{i=1}^{h+g(e)}\models(f,g,\overline{a},\gamma)
where
\mathfrak{p}_{i}:=\mathfrak{p}
and
I_{i}:=R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
h<i\leq g(e)
. So
(f,g,\overline{a},\gamma)\in V
if and only if
(f,\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma)\in V
. So we are now in the situation of case
Y=\emptyset
.

If
\gamma\prod_{j=1}^{n}e_{j}\in\textnormal{rad}eR
then there exist
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
r\in R
such that
(\gamma\prod_{j=1}^{n}e_{j})^{l}=er
. Thus, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}
with
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}
,
|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/eR_{\mathfrak{p}}|\leq\prod_{j=1}^{n}|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/e_{j}R_{\mathfrak{p}}|^{l}
. Therefore
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(f,g,\overline{a},\gamma)
if and only if there exists
f^{\prime}:X\cup\{e\}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
where
f^{\prime}(x)=f(x)
for all
x\in X
and
g(e)\leq f^{\prime}(e)\leq\prod_{j=1}^{n}f(e_{j})^{l}
and
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(f^{\prime},\emptyset,\overline{a},\gamma)
. Since the set of
f^{\prime}
is finite and computable, we are now in the situation of case
Y=\emptyset
.

Case
|Y|\geq 2
:
We show how to reduce to the situation where
|Y|\leq 1
. By
4.3
, we may assume that
g
is a constant function. Take
e_{1},e_{2}\in Y
non-equal. Let
\alpha,r,s\in R
be such that
e_{1}\alpha=e_{2}r
and
e_{2}(\alpha-1)=e_{1}s
. Since for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
, either
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}
or
\alpha-1\notin\mathfrak{p}
, by
4.1
,
(f,g,\overline{a},\gamma)\in V
if and only if

\bigsqcup_{(f_{1},f_{2},g_{1},g_{2})\in\Omega_{f,g,2}}(f_{1},g_{1},\overline{a},\alpha\gamma)\sqcap(f_{2},g_{2},\overline{a},(\alpha-1)\gamma)\in\mathbb{V}.

Note that if
g
is constant then
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are constant for all
(f_{1},f_{2},g_{1},g_{2})\in\Omega_{f,g}
.

For each
(f_{1},f_{2},g_{1},g_{2})\in\Omega_{f,g}
, either
|Y_{1}|<|Y|
or
e_{1},e_{2}\in Y_{1}
. In the first case we are done. In the second,
(f_{1},g_{1},\overline{a},\alpha\gamma)\in V
if and only if
(f_{1},g_{1}|_{Y_{1}\backslash\{e_{1}\}},\overline{a},\alpha\gamma)\in V
. This is because, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}
with
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}
,
|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/e_{1}R_{\mathfrak{p}}|\geq|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/e_{2}R_{\mathfrak{p}}|
and hence if
|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/e_{2}R_{\mathfrak{p}}|\geq g_{1}(e_{2})
then
|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/e_{1}R_{\mathfrak{p}}|\geq g_{1}(e_{2})=g_{1}(e_{1})
. So we may replace
(f_{1},g_{1},\overline{a},\alpha\gamma)
by
(f_{1},g_{1}|_{Y_{1}\backslash\{e_{1}\}},\overline{a},\alpha\gamma)
. A similar argument shows that either
|Y_{2}|<|Y|
or we can replace
(f_{2},g_{2},\overline{a},(\alpha-1)\gamma)
by
(f_{2},g_{2}|_{Y_{1}\backslash\{e_{2}\}},\overline{a},(\alpha-1)\gamma)
.
∎

Corollary 7.5
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain with the radical relation and
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
recursive. There is an algorithm which given
r,a,\gamma,\delta\in R
,
n,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
,
e_{j}\in R
for
1\leq j\leq n
,
e_{j}^{\prime}\in R
for
1\leq j\leq n^{\prime}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
N_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
for
1\leq j\leq n
and
N_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
for
1\leq j\leq n^{\prime}
, answers whether there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that

(1)

(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})\models(r,a,\gamma,\delta)
for
1\leq i\leq h
,

(2)

\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/I_{i}\right|=N
,

(3)

\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/e_{j}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=N_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq n
, and

(4)

\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/e_{j}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|\geq N_{j}^{\prime}
for
1\leq j\leq n^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The proof is as in
7.3
but we use
7.4
in place of
7.2
.
∎

Theorem 7.6
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain. The theory of
R
-modules of size
n
is decidable uniformly
in
n
if and only if
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
is recursive.

Proof.

The forward direction is
3.19
.

Standard arguments using the Baur-Monk theorem and the fact that
T_{R}
is recursively axiomatisable imply that the theory of
R
-modules of size
n
is decidable uniformly in
n
if and only if there is an algorithm which, given
N\in\mathbb{N}
, pairs of pp-
1
-formulae
\varphi_{i}/\psi_{i}
and
A_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
for
1\leq i\leq m
, answers whether there exists
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that satisfying

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{x=0}}\right|=N\wedge\bigwedge_{i=1}^{m}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi_{i}}}{{\psi_{i}}}\right|=A_{i}.

Unfortunately, we can’t directly apply the statement of
[
GLT19
, 4.1]
to reduce to the case that the pp-pairs
\nicefrac{{\varphi_{i}}}{{\psi_{i}}}
are of the form
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}
or
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}
. However, the proof of
[
GLT19
, 4.1]
can be easily modified to allow us to do this. Roughly speaking, starting with a sentence
\chi
of the form

\bigwedge_{i=1}^{l}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi_{i}}}{{\psi_{i}}}\right|=A_{i}\wedge\bigwedge^{m}_{i=l+1}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi_{i}}}{{\psi_{i}}}\right|\geq A_{i}

where
A_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
and
\nicefrac{{\varphi_{i}}}{{\psi_{i}}}
is an arbitrary pp-pair for
1\leq i\leq m
, each step of the proof of
[
GLT19
, 4.1]
produces a finite set
S
of finite tuples of sentences
({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{1},\ldots,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{k})
as in (
7
), but with the form of the pp-pairs involved progressively improved, such that there is an
R
-module satisfying
\chi
if and only there exist
({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{1},\ldots,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{k})\in S
and
R
-modules
M_{i}\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
with
M_{i}\models{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq k
. In order to adapt the proof to our situation, the reader just needs to note that, for each of the steps of the proof of
[
GLT19
, 4.1]
, if the initial sentence
\chi
is as in (
7
), then the sentences
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{i}
in the finite tuples
({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{1},\ldots,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{k})\in S
are of the same form as in (
7
).

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
Z
be a finite set of pp-pairs of the form
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}
and
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}
, and
f:Z\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
. Let
\chi
be the sentence

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{x=0}}\right|=N\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Z}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}).

Recall that every finite module over a Prüfer domain is isomorphic to a direct sum of modules of the form
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I
for some prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideal
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Let
S_{Z}
,
\rho_{Z}
and
s_{Z}
be as in
6.13
with
\lambda:=1
. Enumerate
S_{Z}:=\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{m}\}
and let
q_{i}:=(r_{i},r_{i}a_{i},\gamma_{i},\delta_{i})
. By definition, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
there exists
1\leq i\leq m
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q_{i}
. Therefore, there exists
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
satisfying
\chi
if and only if there exist
N_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
and
f_{i}:Z\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
for
1\leq i\leq m
such that
N=\prod_{i=1}^{m}N_{i}
, for all
\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Z
,
f(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})=\prod_{i=1}^{m}f_{i}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})
and there exist
h_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{ij}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{ij}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}
for
1\leq j\leq h_{i}
such that

(a)
i

(\mathfrak{p}_{ij},I_{ij})\models q_{i}
for
1\leq j\leq h_{i}
and

(b)
i

\bigoplus_{j=1}^{h_{j}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/I_{ij}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{x=0}}\right|=N_{i}\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Z}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}).

Fix
1\leq i\leq m
,
N_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
and
f_{i}
as above. If
r_{i}=0
and
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{i},r_{i}a_{i},\gamma_{i},\delta_{i})
then
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I=R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, which is not finite. Hence, (a)
i
and (b)
i
holds if and only if
h_{i}=0
,
N_{i}=1
and
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})=1
for all
\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Z
. So, we may assume that
r_{i}\neq 0
. Note, by
2.14
4
, if
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I
is finite then
I^{\#}=\mathfrak{p}
. So,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{i},r_{i}a_{i},\gamma_{i},\delta_{i})
if and only if
I=r_{i}J
for some
J\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
a\in J
,
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}
and
\delta\notin\mathfrak{p}
.

Therefore
h_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\mathfrak{p}_{ij}\lhd R
and
I_{ij}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}
for
1\leq j\leq h_{i}
satisfy (a)
i
and (b)
i
if and only if for each
1\leq j\leq h_{i}
, there exists
J_{ij}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}
such that
I_{ij}=r_{i}J_{ij}
,
a\in J_{ij}
,
\gamma\delta\notin\mathfrak{p}
,

\left|\bigoplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/I_{ij}\right|=N_{i}
, and,
for all
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\in Z
,

(1)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=1
then
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/s_{Z}(q_{i},\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})\cdot N_{i}^{-1}

(2)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=2
then
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/s_{Z}(q_{i},\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})\cdot N_{i}

(3)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=3
then
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=1

and for all
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\in Z
,

(1)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=1
then
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=N_{i}

(2)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=2
then
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p_{ij}}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p_{ij}}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})

(3)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=3
then
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}I_{ij}/bI_{ij}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})

(4)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=4
then
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=1

(5)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=5
then
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/s_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})\cdot N_{i}
.

Since
R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/I_{ij}
is finite, by
2.16
, if
b\neq 0
then
\left|I_{ij}/bI_{ij}\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/bR_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|
. By definition,
\rho_{Z}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=3
implies
b\neq 0
. So (
3
) in the second list of conditions may be replaced by

(
3
’)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=3
then
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/bR_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})
.

Finally, the condition that
\left|\bigoplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/I_{ij}\right|=N_{i}
can be replaced by the condition that there exist
N_{i}^{\prime},N_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
N_{i}=N_{i}^{\prime}N_{i}^{\prime\prime}
,
\left|\bigoplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/r_{i}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|=N_{i}^{\prime}
and
\left|\bigoplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/J_{ij}\right|=N_{i}^{\prime\prime}
.
The proof can now be finished using
7.3
.
∎

8.
Removing
|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}|=D
and
|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}|=G
.

This section uses results from sections
6
and
7
. For
d\in R\backslash\{0\}
, respectively
b,c\in R\backslash\{0\}
, we show that there is an algorithm answering whether there exists a sum of modules
\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/dI_{i}
, respectively
\oplus_{i=1}^{h}I_{i}/bcR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
, satisfying a sentence as in
5.1
under the assumption that one of the conjuncts is
\left|d|x/x=0\right|=D
, respectively
\left|xb=0/c|x\right|=G
.
These results will be used in section
10
to eliminate expressions of the form
|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}|=D
and
|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}|=G
, where
D,G\geq 2
.

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain such that
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
and the radical relation are recursive. There exists an algorithm which, given a sentence
\chi
of the form

\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=\ D\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Y}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|\geq g(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}),

where
d\in R\backslash\{0\}
,
D\in\mathbb{N}
,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
and
X,Y
are disjoint finite sets of pp-pairs of the form
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}
and
\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}
, answers whether there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/dI_{i}\models{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}
.

Proof.

Let
Z:=X\cup Y
and let
S_{Z}
,
\rho_{Z}
and
s_{Z}
be as in
6.13
with
\lambda:=d
. Enumerate
S_{Z}:=\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{m}\}
and let
q_{i}:=(r_{i},r_{i}a_{i},\gamma_{i},\delta_{i})
. By definition, for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, there exists
1\leq i\leq m
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models q_{i}
.

Therefore, there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/dI_{i}\models{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}
if and only if there exist
D_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
for
1\leq i\leq m
such that
\prod_{i=1}^{m}D_{i}=D
,
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,m}
and, for
1\leq i\leq m
,
h_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{ij}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{ij}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}
such that, for
1\leq i\leq m
,

(a)
i

(\mathfrak{p}_{ij},I_{ij})\models q_{i}
for
1\leq j\leq h_{i}

(b)
i

\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{j}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/dI_{ij}
satisfies

\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=\ D_{i}\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X_{i}}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Y_{i}}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|\geq g_{i}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}).

Fix
1\leq i\leq m
,
D_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
and
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,m}
as above.
Note that

\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/dI_{ij})\right|=D_{i}\ \text{ if and only if }\ \left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/I_{ij}\right|=D_{i}.

So, exactly as in
7.6
, we may assume
r_{i}\neq 0
for
1\leq i\leq m
. Moreover, by
2.14
4
,
I_{ij}^{\#}=\mathfrak{p}_{ij}
.

Now
h_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\mathfrak{p}_{ij}\lhd R
and
I_{ij}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}
for
1\leq j\leq h_{i}
satisfy (a)
i
if and only if for
1\leq j\leq h_{i}
, there exists
J_{ij}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
such that
I_{ij}=r_{i}J_{ij}
,
a_{i}\in J_{ij}
,
\gamma_{i}\notin\mathfrak{p}_{ij}
and
\delta_{i}\notin J_{ij}^{\#}=I_{ij}^{\#}=\mathfrak{p}_{ij}
.

Now
h_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\mathfrak{p}_{ij}\lhd R
and
J_{ij}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}
with
I_{ij}:=r_{i}J_{ij}
for
1\leq j\leq h_{i}
satisfy (b)
i
if and only if there exists
D^{\prime}_{i},D^{\prime\prime}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
with
D^{\prime}_{i}D^{\prime\prime}_{i}=D_{i}
such that

\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/J_{ij}\right|=D^{\prime}_{i}\ \text{ and }\ \left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/r_{i}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|=D^{\prime\prime}_{i},

for all
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\in X_{i}
(respectively
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\in Y_{i}
),

(1)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=1
then
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=D_{i}
(respectively
D_{i}\geq g_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})
)

(2)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=2
then
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})
(respectively
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|\geq g_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})
)

(3)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=3
then
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/bR_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})
(respectively
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/bR_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|\geq g_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})
)

(4)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=4
then
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=1
(respectively
g_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=1
)

(5)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=5
then
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/s_{Z}(q_{i},\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})\cdot D_{i}
(respectively
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/s_{Z}(q_{i},\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|\geq g_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})\cdot D_{i}
).

and
for all
\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}\in X_{i}
(respectively
\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}}\in Y_{i}
),

(1)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})=1
then
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/s_{Z}(q_{i},\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})\cdot D_{i}^{-1}
(respectively
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/s_{Z}(q_{i},\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|\geq g_{i}(\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})\cdot D_{i}^{-1}
),

(2)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})=2
then
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/s_{Z}(q_{i},\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})\cdot D_{i}
(respectively
\left|\oplus_{j=1}^{h_{i}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/s_{Z}(q_{i},\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|\geq g_{i}(\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})\cdot D_{i}
), and,

(3)

if
\rho_{Z}(q_{i})(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=3
then
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})=1
(respectively
g_{i}(\nicefrac{{a|x}}{{x=0}})=1
).

So we are now done by
7.5
.
∎

Proposition 8.2
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain such that
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
and the radical relation are recursive. There exists an algorithm which, given a sentence
\chi
of the form

\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=\ G\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Y}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|\geq g(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}),

where
b,c\in R\backslash\{0\}
,
G\in\mathbb{N}
,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
and
X,Y
are disjoint finite sets of pp-pairs of the form
\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}
and
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}
, answers whether there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
with
b,c\in I_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
\oplus_{i=1}^{h}I_{i}/bcR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\models{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}
.

Proof.

The proof, which we leave to the reader, is very similar to
8.1
, except we use
6.15
in place of
6.13
and the fact that
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}(I/bcR_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\right|
in place of
\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/dI)\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\right|
.
∎

9.
Further syntactic reductions

In this section we continue work of section
5
to improve the form of the conjunction in
5.1
. Some of these reductions use results in sections
7
and
8
, which only apply to Prüfer domains (i.e. they do not apply to arbitrary arithmetical rings).

Let
W
be the set of
\mathcal{L}_{R}
-sentences of the form

\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\square_{1}D\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\square_{2}E\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Y}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|\geq g(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\Xi

where
\square_{1},\square_{2}\in\{\geq,=,\emptyset\}
,
d,c,b\in R\backslash\{0\}
,
D,E\in\mathbb{N}
,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
,
X,Y
are finite subsets of pp-pairs of the form
\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{x=0}}
and
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}
, and
\Xi
an auxiliary sentence.

Let
V
be the set of
w\in W
such that there exists an
R
-module which satisfies
w
.

Definition 9.1
.

Let
w\in W
be as in
(
9
)
. Define

\displaystyle z_{1}

\displaystyle:=\left|\{\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{x=0}}\in X\ |\ f(\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{x=0}})>1\}\right|,

\displaystyle z_{2}

\displaystyle:=\left|\{\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{x=0}}\in Y\ |\ g(\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{x=0}})>1\}\right|,

\displaystyle z_{3}

\displaystyle:=\left|\{\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\in X\ |\ f(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c^{\prime}|x}})>1\}\right|,\text{ and }

\displaystyle z_{4}

\displaystyle:=\left|\{\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}\in Y\ |\ g(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c^{\prime}|x}})>1\}\right|.

The
short signature
is defined to be the tuple
(\square_{1},\square_{2})
and the
extended signature
,
\mathtt{exsig}\,w
, is defined to be the tuple
((\square_{1},\square_{2}),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
.

We equip the set
\{\geq,=,\emptyset\}
with a total order
\succ
by putting
\geq\ \succ\ =\ \succ\ \emptyset
. We partially order the set of short signatures
\{\geq,=,\emptyset\}^{2}
by setting
(\square_{1},\square_{2})\geq(\square^{\prime}_{1},\square^{\prime}_{2})
whenever
\square_{1}\succeq\square^{\prime}_{1}
and
\square_{2}\succeq\square^{\prime}_{2}
. Finally, we partially order on the set of extended signatures by setting

((\square_{1},\square_{2}),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))\geq((\square^{\prime}_{1},\square^{\prime}_{2}),(z^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}_{2}),(z^{\prime}_{3},z^{\prime}_{4}))

if and only if

(\square_{1},\square_{2})>(\square^{\prime}_{1},\square^{\prime}_{2})
or
(\square_{1},\square_{2})=(\square^{\prime}_{1},\square^{\prime}_{2})
and

•

z_{1}+z_{2}>z^{\prime}_{1}+z^{\prime}_{2}
or
z_{1}+z_{2}=z^{\prime}_{1}+z^{\prime}_{2}
and
z_{2}\geq z^{\prime}_{2}
, and

•

z_{3}+z_{4}>z^{\prime}_{3}+z^{\prime}_{4}
or
z_{3}+z_{4}=z^{\prime}_{3}+z^{\prime}_{4}
and
z_{4}\geq z^{\prime}_{4}
.

We now present various algorithms which, given
w\in W
of a particular form, output
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
such that
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
, and,
\mathtt{exsig}\,\underline{w}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
. By convention, we give both
\top\in\mathbb{W}
and
\bot\in\mathbb{W}
extended signature
((\emptyset,\emptyset),(0,0),(0,0))
.

Remark 9.2
.

The order on the set of extended signatures is artinian.

As in §
5
, given
w\in W
, we may always assume that
w
is of the form

{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi

where
X,Y
are disjoint finite sets of pp-pairs of the form
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}
or
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}
,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
and
\Xi
is an auxiliary sentence.

Remark 9.3
.

If
w\in W
has extended signature
((\square_{1},\square_{2}),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
then
Dw
has extended signature
((\square_{1},\square_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}),(z_{1},z_{2}))
where
D
denotes the duality defined in
2.3
.

Remark 9.4
.

Let
X,Y
be disjoint finite subsets of pp-pairs,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
and
\Xi
an auxiliary sentence be such that

{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi=\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Y}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|\geq g(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\Xi\in W.

Then, for each
(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{1},\ldots,g_{n})\in\Omega_{f,g,n}
and
1\leq i\leq n
,

{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi=\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in X_{i}}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\bigwedge_{\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\in Y_{i}}\left|\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}}\right|\geq g_{i}(\nicefrac{{\varphi}}{{\psi}})\wedge\Xi\in W,

and,
\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi\leq\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi
. Moreover, for each
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,n}
and
1\leq i\leq n
, either
X=X_{i}
and
Y=Y_{i}
, or,
\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi<{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi
.

Proof.

Fix
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,n}
and
1\leq i\leq n
. Let
((\square_{1},\square_{2}),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
be the extended signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi
and
((\square^{\prime}_{1},\square^{\prime}_{2}),(z^{\prime}_{1},z^{\prime}_{2}),(z^{\prime}_{3},z^{\prime}_{4}))
the extended signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi
. Note that since
X
and
Y
are disjoint, so are
X_{i}=X\cup(Y\backslash Y_{i})
and
Y_{i}
.

That the short signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}
is less than the short signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
follows from the fact that
Y_{i}\subseteq Y
and
X_{i}=X\cup(Y\backslash Y_{i})
.

We show that
z_{1}+z_{2}\geq z_{1}^{\prime}+z_{2}^{\prime}
and
z_{2}\geq z_{2}^{\prime}
. By definition,
z_{1}+z_{2}
is the number of
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in X\cup Y
and
z_{2}
is the number of
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in Y
. Since
g_{i}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})=g(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})>1
for all
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in Y_{i}
, we see that
z_{2}^{\prime}
is the number of
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in Y_{i}
. So
z_{2}^{\prime}\leq z_{2}
because
Y_{i}\subseteq Y
. Since
X
and
Y
are disjoint, so are
X_{i}=X\cup(Y\backslash Y_{i})
and
Y_{i}
. Therefore
z_{1}^{\prime}+z_{2}^{\prime}
is equal to the number of
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in X_{i}\cup Y_{i}=X\cup Y
with
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in Y_{i}
, or,
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in X_{i}
and
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})>1
. Since
X\cup Y=X_{i}\cup Y_{i}
,
z_{1}+z_{2}\geq z_{1}^{\prime}+z_{2}^{\prime}
. A similar argument shows that
z_{3}+z_{4}\geq z_{3}^{\prime}+z_{4}^{\prime}
and
z_{4}\geq z_{4}^{\prime}
. So the extended signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
is less than the extended signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}
.

We now prove the moreover. Since
X,Y
are disjoint,
X\neq X_{i}
if and only if
Y\neq Y_{i}
. Suppose
X\neq X_{i}
. Then there exists
\varphi/\psi\in Y\backslash Y_{i}
. By assumption,
g(\varphi/\psi)>1
.

If
\varphi/\psi
is
d|x/x=0
then the short signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
is
(\geq,\square)
and the short signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}
is
(=,\square^{\prime})
or
(\emptyset,\square^{\prime})
, and, by what we have already proved,
\square^{\prime}\leq\square
. So the short signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}
is strictly less than the short signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
. The case of
xb=0/c|x
for
b,c\neq 0
is similar.

If
\varphi/\psi
is
x=x/c|x
then

\left|\{x=x/c^{\prime}|x\in Y_{i}\ |\ g_{i}(x=x/c^{\prime}|x)>1\}\right|<\left|\{x=x/c^{\prime}|x\in Y\}\right|.

So
\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}<\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
. The case of
\varphi/\psi
equal to
xb=0/x=0
is similar.
∎

9.1.
Reducing the short signature

Proposition 9.5
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain with
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
recursive. There is an algorithm which given
w\in W
with short signature
(=,\square)
or
(\square,=)
, for some
\square\in\{\emptyset,=,\geq\}
, outputs
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
such that
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
, and,
\underline{w}
is a lattice combination of elements
w^{\prime}\in W
such that the short signature of
w^{\prime}
is strictly less than the short signature of
w
.

Proof.

Suppose
w
has short signature
(=,\square)
. Then
w
is of the form

\left|d|x/x=0\right|=D\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi

where
d\in R\backslash\{0\}
,
D\in\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
X
and
Y
are disjoint finite subsets of
\{x=x/c^{\prime}|x,xb^{\prime}=0/x=0\ |\ c^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in R\}\cup\{xb=0/c|x\}
for some
b,c\in R\backslash\{0\}
, and
\Xi
is an auxiliary sentence.

If
M\models w
then, by
2.11
and
6.1
, there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
and
M^{\prime}\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that
M\equiv M^{\prime}\oplus\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/dI_{i}
and
\left|d|x/x=0(M^{\prime})\right|=1
.

Thus there exists
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that
M\models w
if and only if there exist
(f_{1},f_{2},g_{1},g_{2})\in\Omega_{f,g,2}
,
h\in\mathbb{N}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
, such that
\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/dI_{i}
satisfies

w_{(f_{1},f_{2},g_{1},g_{2})}:=\left|d|x/x=0\right|=D\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}}\wedge\Xi

and
M^{\prime}
which satisfies

w^{\prime}_{(f_{1},f_{2},g_{1},g_{2})}:=\left|d|x/x=0\right|=1\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\Xi.

Let
\Omega\subseteq\Omega_{f,g,2}
be the set of
(f_{1},f_{2},g_{1},g_{2})\in\Omega_{f,g,2}
, such that there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
, such that
\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/dI_{i}
satisfies
w_{(f_{1},f_{2},g_{1},g_{2})}
. So
w\in V
if and only if

\underline{w}:=\bigsqcup_{(f_{1},f_{2},g_{1},g_{2})\in\Omega}w^{\prime}_{(f_{1},f_{2},g_{1},g_{2})}\in\mathbb{V}.

By
8.1
, given
w
, we can compute
\Omega
, and, so, we can compute
\underline{w}
. The short signature of each
w^{\prime}_{(f_{1},f_{2},g_{1},g_{2})}
is
(\emptyset,\square^{\prime})
for some
\square^{\prime}\in\{\emptyset,=,\leq\}
and, by
9.4
,
\square\succeq\square^{\prime}
. So
(=,\square)>(\emptyset,\square^{\prime})
as required.

Suppose
w
has short signature
(\square,=)
. Then
w
is

\left|xb=0/c|x\right|=E\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi

where
b,c\in R\backslash\{0\}
,
E\in\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
X
and
Y
are disjoint finite subsets of
\{x=x/c^{\prime}|x,xb^{\prime}=0/x=0\ |\ c^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in R\}\cup\{d|x/x=0\}
for some
d\in R\backslash\{0\}
, and
\Xi
is an auxiliary sentence.

If
M\models w
then, by
2.11
and
6.2
, there exist
M_{1},M_{2},M^{\prime}\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that
b\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}M_{1}
,
c\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}M_{2}
and
\left|xb=0/c|x(M^{\prime})\right|=1
and there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
b,c\in I_{i}
and

\oplus_{i=1}^{h}I_{i}/bcR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\oplus M_{1}\oplus M_{2}\oplus M^{\prime}\models w.

Therefore,
w\in V
if and only if there exist
E_{1},E_{2},E_{4}\in\mathbb{N}
with
E_{1}E_{2}E_{4}=E
and
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,4}
such that

w_{1,E_{1},f_{1},g_{1}}:=\left|x=x/c|x\right|=E_{1}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\left|b|x/x=0\right|=1\in V,

w_{2,E_{2},f_{2},g_{2}}:=\left|xb=0/x=0\right|=E_{2}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\left|c|x/x=0\right|=1\in V,

w_{3,f_{3},g_{3}}:={\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{3},g_{3}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\left|xb=0/c|x\right|=1\in V

and
there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
b,c\in I_{i}
and

\oplus_{i=1}^{h}I_{i}/bcR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\models\left|xb=0/c|x\right|=E_{4}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{4},g_{4}}\wedge\Xi.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be the set of
(E_{1},E_{2},(\overline{f},\overline{g}))\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\times\Omega_{f,g,4}
such that there exists
E_{4}
with
E_{1}E_{2}E_{4}=E
and there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
b,c\in I_{i}
and

\oplus_{i=1}^{h}I_{i}/bcR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\models\left|xb=0/c|x\right|=E_{4}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{4},g_{4}}\wedge\Xi.

Then
w\in V
if and only if

\underline{w}:=\bigsqcup_{(E_{1},E_{2},(\overline{f},\overline{g}))\in\mathcal{H}}w_{1,E_{1},f_{1},g_{1}}\sqcap w_{2,E_{2},f_{3},g_{3}}\sqcap w_{3,f_{3},g_{3}}\in V.

By
8.2
, given
w
, we can compute
\mathcal{H}
and so we can compute
\underline{w}
.

Now if
w
has short signature
(\square,=)
then
w_{1,E_{1},f_{1},g_{1}},w_{2,E_{2},f_{3},g_{3}}
and
w_{3,f_{3},g_{3}}
have short signature
(\square^{\prime},\emptyset)
and by
9.4
,
\square^{\prime}\preceq\square
. So
(\square^{\prime},\emptyset)<(\square,=)
, as required.
∎

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
R
be an arithmetical ring. Let
c,d\in R
and
D\in\mathbb{N}
. For all
C\in\mathbb{N}_{2}
,

T_{R}\models\left|\nicefrac{{xc^{D-1}d=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq D\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\\
\leftrightarrow\left|\nicefrac{{xc^{D-1}d=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C

and

T_{R}\models\left|\nicefrac{{c^{D}d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq D\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\\
\leftrightarrow\bigvee_{D\leq E\leq C^{D}}\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=E\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C.

Proof.

Suppose
M\models\left|\nicefrac{{xc^{D-1}d=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=1
. Then
xc^{i}d=0\leq_{M}c|x
for
0\leq i\leq D-1
. So, by
5.7
, for
0\leq i\leq D-1
,

\left|\nicefrac{{c^{i}d|x}}{{c^{i+1}d|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(M)\right|.

Hence

\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(M)\right|^{D}\left|\nicefrac{{c^{D}d|x}}{{x=0}}(M)\right|.

Therefore, if
C\geq 2
then

T_{R}\models\left|\nicefrac{{xc^{D-1}d=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq D\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\leftrightarrow\\
\left|\nicefrac{{xc^{D-1}d=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C.

Now suppose that
c^{D}d\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}M
. Then
c^{D}d|x
is equivalent to
x=0
in
M
. Therefore, by
5.7
,

\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{c^{D}d|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c^{D}|x+xd=0}}(M)\right|\leq\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c^{D}|x}}(M)\right|\leq\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|^{D}.

Hence

T_{R}\models\left|c^{D}d|x/x=0\right|=1\wedge\left|d|x/x=0\right|\geq D\wedge\left|x=x/c|x\right|=C\leftrightarrow\\
\bigvee_{D\leq E\leq C^{D}}\left|d|x/x=0\right|=E\wedge\left|x=x/c|x\right|=C.\qed

Proposition 9.7
.

Let
R
be a recursive arithmetical ring. There is an algorithm, which given
w\in W
with extended signature
((\geq,\square),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
z_{1}\geq 1
or
z_{3}\geq 1
, returns
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
such that
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
, and,
\mathtt{exsig}\,\underline{w}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
.

Proof.

Suppose
w:={\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi\in W
, where
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
X,Y
are disjoint finite sets of appropriate pp-pairs and
\Xi
is an auxiliary sentence, has extended signature
((\geq,\square),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
z_{1}\geq 1
. Then
\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\in Y
for some
d\in R\backslash\{0\}
and
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in X
for some
c\in R
. Let
D=g(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})
.

Then, by
2.9
,
w\in V
if and only if

\bigsqcup_{(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,2}}\left({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xc^{D-1}d=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi\right)\sqcap\left({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{c^{D}d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi\right)\in\mathbb{V}.

For each
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,2}
, we define

\underline{w}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}},\underline{w}^{\prime}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}\in\mathbb{W}
such that

\mathtt{exsig}\,\underline{w}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}},\mathtt{exsig}\,\underline{w}^{\prime}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w,

\underline{w}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}\in\mathbb{V}\ \text{ if and only if }\ {\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xc^{D-1}d=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi\in V,\text{ and,}

\underline{w}^{\prime}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}\in\mathbb{V}\ \text{ if and only if }\ {\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{c^{D}d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi\in V.

Once we have done this, the proof is complete since then

\underline{w}:=\bigsqcup_{(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,2}}\underline{w}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}\sqcap\underline{w}^{\prime}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}

has the properties required by the statement.

If
\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}}<\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
then set
\underline{w}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}:={\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xc^{D-1}d=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi
. Otherwise, by
9.4
,
X=X_{1}
and
Y=Y_{1}
. Further,
f_{1}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})>1
and, by definition of
\Omega_{f,g,2}
,
g_{1}(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=g(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=D
. Let
Y_{1}^{\prime}:=Y_{1}\backslash\{\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\}
and
g_{1}^{\prime}:=g_{1}|_{Y_{1}^{\prime}}
. Then, by
9.6
,

{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xc^{D-1}d=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi\in V

if and only if

\underline{w}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}:={\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{1},g_{1}^{\prime}}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xc^{D-1}d=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi\in\mathbb{V}.

Moreover,
\underline{w}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}
has short signature
(\emptyset,\square^{\prime})
, where, by
9.4
,
\square^{\prime}\preceq\square
. Therefore
\mathtt{exsig}\,\underline{w}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
because
(\emptyset,\square^{\prime})<(\emptyset,\square)
.

If
\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}<\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
then let
\underline{w}^{\prime}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}:={\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{c^{D}d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi
. Otherwise, by
9.4
,
X=X_{1}
and
Y=Y_{1}
. Further,
f_{2}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})>1
and, by definition of
\Omega_{f,g,2}
,
g_{2}(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=g(\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}})=D
.

Let
X_{2}^{\prime}:=X_{2}\backslash\{\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\}
and
Y_{2}^{\prime}:=Y_{2}\backslash\{\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\}
. Let
f_{2}^{\prime}:=f_{2}|_{X_{2}^{\prime}}
,
g_{2}^{\prime}:=g_{2}|_{Y_{2}^{\prime}}
and
C:=f_{2}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})
. Then
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{c^{D}d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi
is

\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq D\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2}^{\prime},g_{2}^{\prime}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{c^{D}d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1.

Let

\underline{w}^{\prime}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}:=\bigsqcup_{D\leq E\leq C^{D}}\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=E\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2}^{\prime},g_{2}^{\prime}}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{c^{D}d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi.

By
9.6
,
\underline{w}^{\prime}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}\in\mathbb{V}
if and only if
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2},g_{2}}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{c^{D}d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi\in V
. The short signature of each component of the join defining
\underline{w}^{\prime}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}
is
(=,\square^{\prime})
where, by
9.4
,
\square^{\prime}\preceq\square
. Therefore
\mathtt{exsig}\,\underline{w}^{\prime}_{\overline{f},\overline{g}}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
because
(=,\square^{\prime})<(\geq,\square)
.

Suppose
w\in W
has extended signature
((\geq,\square),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
z_{3}\geq 1
. Then
Dw
has extended signature
((\geq,\square),(z_{3},z_{4}),(z_{1},z_{2}))
. By the previous case, we can compute
\underline{w}
, a lattice combination of elements of
W
with extended signatures strictly less than
Dw
such that
Dw\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
. Now
w\in V
if and only if
D\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
and
\mathtt{exsig}\,D\underline{w}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
.
∎

Lemma 9.8
.

Let
R
be a Prüfer domain. Let
a,b,c\in R
and
E,C\in\mathbb{N}_{2}
. Suppose that
r,s,\alpha\in R
are such that
c\alpha=ar
and
a(\alpha-1)=cs
. Define

(1)

\Sigma_{1}
to be the formula
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\alpha-1|x}}\right|=1

(2)

\Sigma_{2}
to be the formula
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\alpha|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{ar|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1

(3)

\Sigma_{3}
to be the formula
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\alpha|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xr=0}}{{a|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{rb|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1

(4)

\Sigma_{4}
to be the formula
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\alpha|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xr=0}}{{a|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{r|x}}\right|=1
.

Then for all
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
there exist
M_{1},\ldots,M_{4}\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that
M_{i}\models\Sigma_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq 4
and
M\equiv M_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus M_{4}
.

(1)

If
i\in\{1,4\}
and
C<E
then

T_{R}\models\lnot(\Sigma_{i}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\geq E),

and if
i\in\{1,4\}
and
E\leq C
then

T_{R}\models\Sigma_{i}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\geq E\leftrightarrow\\
\bigvee_{E\leq E^{\prime}\leq C}\Sigma_{i}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=E^{\prime}.

(2)

In
T_{R}
the following equivalence holds.

\Sigma_{2}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\geq E\leftrightarrow\Sigma_{2}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq E

(3)

In
T_{R}
the following equivalence holds.

\Sigma_{3}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\geq E\leftrightarrow\Sigma_{3}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{r|x}}\right|\geq\lceil E/C\rceil

Proof.

The first claim follows from
2.9
.

(i) Suppose
M\models\Sigma_{1}
. Then

\left|x=x/a|x(M)\right|=\left|x=x/cs|x(M)\right|\geq\left|xb=0/c|x(M)\right|.

Suppose
M\models\Sigma_{4}
. Then

\left|r|x/ar|x(M)\right|=\left|x=x/xr=0+a|x(M)\right|=\left|x=x/a|x(M)\right|

because
\left|xr=0/a|x(M)\right|=1
and hence
xr=0\leq_{M}a|x
.
So

\left|xb=0/c|x(M)\right|=\left|xb=0/ar|x(M)\right|\leq\left|r|x/ar|x(M)\right|=\left|x=x/a|x(M)\right|.

The first equality holds because
\left|x=x/\alpha|x(M)\right|=1
and the inequality holds because
\left|xb=0/r|x\right|=1
and hence
xb=0\leq_{M}r|x
.

Therefore, if
M\models\Sigma_{i}
for
i\in\{1,4\}
then

\left|xb=0/c|x(M)\right|\leq\left|x=x/a|x(M)\right|.

The first claim follows from this.

(ii) Suppose
M\models\Sigma_{2}
. Then
c|x
is equivalent to
ar|x
in
M
and
ar|x
is equivalent to
x=0
in
M
. So
\left|xb=0/c|x(M)\right|=\left|xb=0/x=0(M)\right|
.

(iii) Suppose
M\models\Sigma_{3}
. Then

\left|xb=0/c|x(M)\right|=\left|xb=0/ar|x(M)\right|=\left|xb=0/r|x(M)\right|\cdot\left|r|x/ar|x(M)\right|.

Since
\left|xr=0/a|x(M)\right|=1
, by
5.7
,
\left|r|x/ar|x(M)\right|=\left|x=x/a|x(M)\right|
. The claim now follows.
∎

Proposition 9.9
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain. There is algorithm which given
w\in W
with extended signature
((\square,\geq),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
z_{1}\geq 1
or
z_{3}\geq 1
outputs
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
such that
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
and
\mathtt{exsig}\,\underline{w}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
.

Proof.

For
w\in W
as in (
9
), define
\deg w=E
. Given
w\in W
with extended signature
((\square,\geq),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
z_{1}\geq 1
, we will show how to compute
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
, a lattice combination of
w^{\prime}\in W
, such that for each
w^{\prime}
, either
\mathtt{exsig}\,w^{\prime}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
or
\mathtt{exsig}\,w^{\prime}=\mathtt{exsig}\,w
and
\deg w^{\prime}<\deg w
, and, such that
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
. Since
\deg
takes values in
\mathbb{N}
, by iterating this process we will eventually compute
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
such that
\mathtt{exsig}\,\underline{w}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
.

Suppose
w:={\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi\in W
, where
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
X,Y
are disjoint finite sets of appropriate pp-pairs and
\Xi
is an auxiliary sentence, has extended signature
((\square,\geq),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
z_{1}\geq 1
. Since
w
has extended signature
((\square,\geq),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
z_{1}\geq 1
, there exist
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\in X
and
\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\in Y
.

Let
\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2},\Sigma_{3},\Sigma_{4}
be as in
9.8
. Then
w\in V
if and only if

\bigsqcup_{(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,4}}\bigsqcap_{i=1}^{4}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{i}\in\mathbb{V}.

We show that for each
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,4}
, either the extended signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi
is strictly less than the extended signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi
or we will show that we can compute
\underline{w}_{i,(\overline{f},\overline{g})}\in\mathbb{W}
such that
\underline{w}_{i,(\overline{f},\overline{g})}
is a lattice combination of
w^{\prime}\in W
with either
\mathtt{exsig}\,w^{\prime}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
, or,
\mathtt{exsig}\,w^{\prime}\leq\mathtt{exsig}\,w
and
\deg w^{\prime}<w
.

Fix
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,4}
and
1\leq i\leq 4
. By
9.4
,
\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi<\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi
unless
X_{i}=X
and
Y_{i}=Y
. Moreover, if
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}})=1
then
\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi<\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi
. So we may assume
X_{i}=X
,
Y_{i}=Y
and
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}})>1
.

Let
X^{\prime}:=X\backslash\{\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\}
,
Y^{\prime}:=Y\backslash\{\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|X}}\}
,
f_{i}^{\prime}:=f_{i}|_{X^{\prime}}
,
g_{i}^{\prime}:=g_{i}|_{Y^{\prime}}
,
C:=f_{i}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}})
and
E:=g_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})=g(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})
. Then
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi
is

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\geq E\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i}^{\prime},g_{i}^{\prime}}\wedge\Xi.

Case
i=1
or
i=4
:

If
f_{1}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}})<g_{1}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})
then, by
9.8
,

T_{R}\models\lnot({\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{i}).

So set
\underline{w}_{i,(\overline{f},\overline{g})}:=\bot
.

Suppose
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}})\geq g_{i}(\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}})
. Then, by
9.8
,

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\geq E\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i}^{\prime},g_{i}^{\prime}}\wedge\Xi\in V

if and only

\underline{w}_{i,(\overline{f},\overline{g})}:=\bigsqcup_{E\leq E^{\prime}\leq C}\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|=E^{\prime}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i}^{\prime},g_{i}^{\prime}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{i}\in\mathbb{V}.

So we are done since the short signatures of the components of the join defining
\underline{w}_{i,(\overline{f},\overline{g})}
are
(\square^{\prime},=)
where, by
9.4
,
\square^{\prime}\preceq\square
.

Case
i=2
:

By
9.8
,

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\geq E\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2}^{\prime},g_{2}^{\prime}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2}\in V

if and only

\underline{w}_{i,(\overline{f},\overline{g})}:=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq E\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2}^{\prime},g_{2}^{\prime}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2}\in V.

So we are done since the short signature of
\underline{w}_{i,(\overline{f},\overline{g})}
is
(\square^{\prime},\emptyset)
where, by
9.4
,
\square^{\prime}\preceq\square
.

Case
i=3
:

By
9.8
,

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\geq E\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2}^{\prime},g_{2}^{\prime}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{2}\in V

if and only

\underline{w}_{i,(\overline{f},\overline{g})}:=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{r|x}}\right|\geq\lceil E/C\rceil\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{2}^{\prime},g_{2}^{\prime}}\wedge\Xi\wedge\Sigma_{3}\in V.

Since
\lceil E/C\rceil<E
, we have
\deg\underline{w}_{i,(\overline{f},\overline{g})}<E=\deg{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi
.

So we have proved the lemma for the case
z_{1}\geq 1
. Suppose
w\in W
has extended signature
((\square,\geq),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
z_{3}\geq 1
. Then
Dw
has extended signature
((\square,\geq),(z_{3},z_{4}),(z_{1},z_{2}))
. So by the version of the lemma just proved, we can compute
\underline{w}\in W
with
\mathtt{exsig}\,\underline{w}<\mathtt{exsig}\,Dw
such that
Dw\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
. Now,
w\in V
if and only if
D\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
and
\mathtt{exsig}\,D\underline{w}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
, as required.
∎

9.2.
Reducing the extended signature

Lemma 9.10
.

Let
a,b\in R
and
A,B\in\mathbb{N}_{2}
. Suppose that
r,s,\alpha\in R
are such that
a\alpha=br
and
b(\alpha-1)=as
. Define

(1)

\Sigma_{1}
to be the sentence
|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\alpha|x}}|=1\wedge|\nicefrac{{rb|x}}{{x=0}}|=1\wedge|\nicefrac{{b^{A}|x}}{{x=0}}|=1
,

(2)

\Sigma_{2}
to be the sentence
|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\alpha|x}}|=1\wedge|\nicefrac{{rb|x}}{{x=0}}|=1\wedge|\nicefrac{{xb^{A-1}=0}}{{b|x}}|=1
,

(3)

\Sigma_{3}
to be the sentence
|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\alpha|x}}|=1\wedge|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{r|x}}|=1
,

(4)

\Sigma_{4}
to be the sentence
|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{(\alpha-1)|x}}|=1\wedge|\nicefrac{{sa|x}}{{x=0}}|=1\wedge|\nicefrac{{a^{B}|x}}{{x=0}}|=1
,

(5)

\Sigma_{5}
to be the sentence
|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{(\alpha-1)|x}}|=1\wedge|\nicefrac{{sa|x}}{{x=0}}|=1\wedge|\nicefrac{{xa^{B-1}=0}}{{a|x}}|=1
, and

(6)

\Sigma_{6}
to be the sentence
|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{(\alpha-1)|x}}|=1\wedge|\nicefrac{{xa=0}}{{s|x}}|=1
.

Then, for all
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
, there exist
M_{i}\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
for
1\leq i\leq 6
such that
M\equiv\oplus_{i=1}^{6}M_{i}
and for
1\leq i\leq 6
,
M_{i}\models\Sigma_{i}
.

Moreover, there is an algorithm which, given
a,b,r,s,\alpha,A,B
as above,
1\leq i\leq 6
and
\square,\square^{\prime}\in\{=,\geq\}
, either returns

\bot
, in which case,

T_{R}\models\lnot(\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|\square A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}B\wedge\Sigma_{i})

or returns
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{n}
such that

T_{R}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|\square A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}B\wedge\Sigma_{i}\leftrightarrow\bigvee_{j=1}^{n}(\sigma_{j}\wedge\Sigma_{i})

and each
\sigma_{j}
is either of the form

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a^{\prime}|x}}\right|\square_{j}A^{\prime}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b^{\prime}|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}_{j}B^{\prime},

where
\square_{j},\square^{\prime}_{j}\in\{\emptyset,=,\geq\}
,
\square_{j}\preceq\square
,
\square^{\prime}_{j}\preceq\square^{\prime}
,
a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in R
,
A^{\prime},B^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
and
A^{\prime}B^{\prime}<AB
or of the form

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a^{\prime}|x}}\right|\square_{j}A^{\prime}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b^{\prime}|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}_{j}B^{\prime}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{x=0}}\right|=N,

where
\square_{j},\square^{\prime}_{j}\in\{=,\geq\}
,
a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in R
and
A^{\prime\prime},B^{\prime\prime},N\in\mathbb{N}.

Proof.

Note that

T_{R}\vdash|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\alpha|x}}|=1\wedge|\nicefrac{{rb|x}}{{x=0}}|=1\rightarrow(a|x\leftrightarrow x=0).

Case 1:

\Sigma\in\{\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}\}
and
\square
is
=
.

In this case,
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|\square A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}B\wedge\Sigma
is equivalent to
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{x=0}}\right|=A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}B\wedge\Sigma
as required.

Case 2:

\Sigma\in\{\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}\}
and
\square
is
\geq
and
B\geq A
.

In this case
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|\square A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}B\wedge\Sigma
is equivalent to
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}B\wedge\Sigma
.

Case 3:

\Sigma=\Sigma_{1}
,
\square
is
\geq
and
\square^{\prime}
is
=
.

If
b^{A}\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}M
and
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}(M)\right|=B
then
B\leq\left|M\right|\leq B^{A}
. Thus
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|\geq A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|=B\wedge\Sigma_{1}
is equivalent to

\bigvee_{B^{A}\geq A^{\prime}\geq A}\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{x=0}}\right|=A^{\prime}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|\geq B\wedge\Sigma_{1}

Case 4:

\Sigma=\Sigma_{2}
,
\square
is
\geq
and
\square^{\prime}
is
=
.

Suppose
M
satisfies
\left|\nicefrac{{xb^{A-1}=0}}{{b|x}}\right|=1
. Then
\left|\nicefrac{{b^{i}|x}}{{b^{i+1}|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}(M)\right|
for
i\geq A-1
. So
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b^{A}|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}(M)\right|^{A}
. Thus if
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}(M)\right|\geq 2
then
\left|M\right|\geq A
. Therefore

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|\geq A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|=B\wedge\Sigma_{2}
is equivalent to
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|=B\wedge\Sigma_{2}
.

Case 5:

\Sigma\in\{\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}\}
and
\square
is
\geq
and
B<A
.

If
\square^{\prime}
is
\geq
then

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|\geq A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|\geq B\wedge\Sigma

is equivalent to

(\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|\geq A\vee\bigvee_{B\leq B^{\prime}<A}\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|=B^{\prime})\wedge\Sigma

So we may reduce to the case where
\square^{\prime}
is
=
and hence to either case
3
or
4
at the expense of replacing
B
by
B^{\prime}
with
B\leq B^{\prime}<A
. This is not a problem since in case
3
we show that
\sigma_{j}
has the form
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a^{\prime}|x}}\right|\square_{j}A^{\prime}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b^{\prime}|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}_{j}B^{\prime}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{x=0}}\right|=N
i.e. there is no restriction on
A^{\prime}
or
B^{\prime}
and in case
4
we show that
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|\geq A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|=B^{\prime}\wedge\Sigma_{2}
is equivalent to
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|=B^{\prime}\wedge\Sigma_{2}
and
B^{\prime}\leq A
.

Case 6:

\Sigma=\Sigma_{3}
.

Suppose that
M
satisfies
\Sigma_{3}
. Then
a|x
is equivalent to
br|x
in
M
and

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{br|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}(M)\right|\cdot\left|\nicefrac{{b|x}}{{br|x}}(M)\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}(M)\right|\cdot\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{r|x}}(M)\right|.

So, in this case, the result now follows from
5.6
.

The remaining cases follow from the cases we have already covered by exchanging the roles of
\alpha
and
\alpha-1
,
a
and
b
,
r
and
s
, and
A
and
B
.
∎

Proposition 9.11
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain. There is an algorithm which, given
w\in W
with extended signature
((\square_{1},\square_{2}),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
z_{1}+z_{2}>1
or
z_{3}+z_{4}>1
, outputs
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
such that
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
and
\mathtt{exsig}\,\underline{w}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
.

Proof.

For
w\in W
, as in (
9
), define

\deg_{1}w:=\prod_{\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in X}f(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})\cdot\prod_{\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in Y}g(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}).

Given
w\in W
with extended signature
((\square_{1},\square_{2}),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
z_{1}+z_{2}>1
, we will show how to compute
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
, a lattice combination of
w^{\prime}\in W
, such that
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
and such that for each
w^{\prime}
, either
\mathtt{exsig}\,w^{\prime}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
, or,
\mathtt{exsig}\,w^{\prime}\leq\mathtt{exsig}\,w
and
\deg_{1}w^{\prime}<\deg_{1}w
. Since
\deg_{1}
takes values in
\mathbb{N}
, by iterating this process, we will eventually compute
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
which is a lattice combination of
w^{\prime}\in W
such that
\mathtt{exsig}\,w^{\prime}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
.

We start with a special case. Let
a,b,\alpha,r,s\in R
be such that
a\alpha=br
and
b(\alpha-1)=as
. Let
\Sigma_{i}
, for
1\leq i\leq 6
, be as in
9.10
. Suppose that, for some
1\leq i\leq 6
,
w
is

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|\square A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}B\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi\in W

where
A,B\in\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
\square,\square^{\prime}\in\{=,\geq\}
,
X
and
Y
are finite sets of appropriate pp-pairs,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
and
\Xi
is an auxiliary sentence.

We will compute
u=\bigsqcup_{j=1}^{n}u_{j}
such that for each
1\leq j\leq n
, either
\mathtt{exsig}\,u_{j}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
, or,
\mathtt{exsig}\,u_{j}\leq\mathtt{exsig}\,w
and
\deg_{1}u_{j}<\deg_{1}w
.

If the algorithm from
9.10
returns
\bot
, then

T_{R}\models\lnot(\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|\square A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}B\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi).

In this case, set
u:=\bot
. Otherwise, let
\sigma_{1},\ldots\sigma_{n}
be, as in
9.10
, such that

T_{R}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|\square A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}B\wedge\Sigma_{i}\leftrightarrow\bigvee_{j=1}^{n}(\sigma_{j}\wedge\Sigma_{i}).

Therefore

T_{R}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}}\right|\square A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\right|\square^{\prime}B\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi\leftrightarrow\bigvee_{j=1}^{n}(\sigma_{j}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi)

So
w\in V
if and only if

\bigsqcup_{j=1}^{n}(\sigma_{j}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi)\in\mathbb{V}.

If
\sigma_{j}
is of the form

|x=x/a^{\prime}|x|\square_{j}A^{\prime}\wedge|x=x/b^{\prime}|x|\square^{\prime}_{j}B^{\prime}\wedge|x=x/x=0|=N,

where
\square_{j},\square^{\prime}_{j}\in\{=,\geq\}
,
a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in R
and
A^{\prime\prime},B^{\prime\prime},N\in\mathbb{N}
then
\sigma_{j}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi
is a sentence about an
R
-module of fixed finite size. So, since
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
is recursive, by
7.6
, we can effectively decide whether
\sigma_{j}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi
holds in some
R
-module. Set
u_{j}:=\top
if
\sigma_{j}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi
is true in some
R
-module and
u_{j}:=\bot
otherwise. Finally, if
\sigma_{j}
is of the form

|x=x/a^{\prime}|x|\square_{j}A^{\prime}\wedge|x=x/b^{\prime}|x|\square^{\prime}_{j}B^{\prime},

where
\square_{j},\square^{\prime}_{j}\in\{\emptyset,=,\geq\}
,
a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in R
and
A^{\prime},B^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
with
\square_{j}\preceq\square
,
\square^{\prime}_{j}\preceq\square^{\prime}
and
A^{\prime}B^{\prime}<AB
then set
u_{j}:=\sigma_{j}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi
. The condition that
\square_{j}\preceq\square
and
\square^{\prime}_{j}\preceq\square^{\prime}
ensures that
\mathtt{exsig}\,u_{j}\leq\mathtt{exsig}\,w
. The condition that
A^{\prime}B^{\prime}<AB
implies that
\deg_{1}u_{j}<\deg_{1}w
. So
w\in V
if and only if
u:=\bigsqcup_{j=1}^{n}u_{j}\in\mathbb{V}
and
\deg_{1}u_{j}<\deg_{1}w
for
1\leq j\leq n
as required.

We now consider the general case. Suppose

w:={\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi,

where
X
and
Y
are disjoint finite sets of appropriate pp-pairs,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}_{2}
and
\Xi
is an auxiliary sentence, has extended signature
((\square_{1},\square_{2}),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
z_{1}+z_{2}>1
. There exist
a,b\in R
with
a\neq b
such that
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{a|x}},\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{b|x}}\in X\cup Y
. Then
w\in V
if and only if

\bigsqcup_{(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,6}}\bigsqcap_{i=1}^{6}\Sigma_{i}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi\in\mathbb{V}.

Claim:
For all
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,6}
and
1\leq i\leq 6
, either

\mathtt{exsig}\,\Sigma_{i}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi<\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi,\text{ or, }\deg_{1}\Sigma_{i}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi<\deg_{1}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g},

or,
X_{i}=X
,
Y_{i}=Y
,
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})=f(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})
for all
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in X
and
g_{i}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})=g(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})
for all
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in Y
.

By
9.4
, if
X_{i}\neq X
or
Y_{i}\neq Y
then the extended signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}
is strictly less that the extended signature of
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
. So suppose that
X=X_{i}
,
Y=Y_{i}
. By definition,
g(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})=g_{i}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})
for all
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in Y=Y_{i}
and
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})\leq f(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})
for all
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in X=X_{i}
. So, if
f_{i}(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})<f(\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}})
, for some
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\in X
, then
\deg_{1}\Sigma_{i}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi<\deg_{1}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}
. So we have proved the claim.

Therefore for each
(\overline{f},\overline{g})\in\Omega_{f,g,6}
and
1\leq i\leq 6
, either
\mathtt{exsig}\,\Sigma_{i}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi<\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi
,
\deg_{1}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi<\deg_{1}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi
or
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Sigma_{i}\wedge\Xi
is of the form of the special case. Since, by
9.4
, we always have
\mathtt{exsig}\,\Sigma_{i}\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f_{i},g_{i}}\wedge\Xi\leq\mathtt{exsig}\,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi
, we are done.

The version of the lemma with
z_{3}+z_{4}>1
follows from the one we have just proved by applying duality as in
9.9
.
∎

Say
w\in W
is
reducible
if
w
has

•

short signature
(=,\square)
or
(\square,=)
, or

•

extended signature
((\geq,\square),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
or
((\square,\geq),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
z_{1}\geq 1
or
z_{3}\geq 1
, or

•

extended signature
((\square,\square^{\prime}),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
z_{1}+z_{2}>1
or
z_{3}+z_{4}>1
.

Remark 9.12
.

If
w\in W
is reducible then
w
is of the form required by one of the lemmas
9.5
,
9.7
,
9.9
or
9.11
.

Thus
w\in W
is not reducible if and only if
w
has extended signature

•

(\emptyset,\emptyset,(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4})\in\{(1,0),(0,1),(0,0)\}
or

•

((\square,\square^{\prime}),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
(\square,\square^{\prime})\in\{(\geq,\emptyset),(\emptyset,\geq),(\geq,\geq),(\emptyset,\emptyset)\}
and

(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4})\in\{(0,1),(0,0)\}
.

The next remark follows directly from
3.9
.

Remark 9.13
.

Let
R
be a Prüfer domain. There is an algorithm which, given
w\in W
with extended signature
((\square_{1},\square_{2}),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))
with
(\square_{1},\square_{2})\in\{(\geq,\emptyset),(\emptyset,\geq),(\geq,\geq),(\emptyset,\emptyset),\}
and
(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4})\in\{(0,1),(0,0)\}
, answers whether
w\in V
or not.

10.
Algorithms for sentences which are not reducible

Lemma 10.1
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain with
X(R)
recursive. There is an algorithm which, given
\lambda\in R
,
C\in\mathbb{N}
and
(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)\in R^{4}
, answers whether there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
and
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=C
.

Proof.

If
C=1
then there is nothing to prove, so suppose that
C\neq 1
. Let
p_{1},\ldots,p_{l}\in\mathbb{P}
be distinct primes and
n_{1},\ldots,n_{l}\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
C=\prod_{j=1}^{l}p_{j}^{n_{j}}
. For each prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
, if
\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|
is finite then it is a prime power. Therefore, there exist prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
and
\left|\oplus_{i}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=C
if and only if for each
1\leq j\leq l
, there exist
h_{j}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{ij}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{ij}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}
for
1\leq i\leq h_{j}
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{ij},I_{ij})\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
and
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h_{j}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|=p^{n_{j}}
. Thus we may reduce to the case that
C=p^{n}
for some
p\in\mathbb{P}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

We consider the cases
r=0
and
r\neq 0
separately.

Case
r=0
:

For
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
prime and
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(0,0,\gamma,\delta)
if and only if
I=0
,
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}
and
\delta\neq 0
. So, there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})\models(0,0,\gamma,\delta)
and
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=p^{n}
if and only if
(p,n;\lambda,\gamma,0,1)\in X(R)
and
\delta\neq 0
.

Case
r\neq 0
:

If
r\neq 0
then
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
if and only if
I=rJ
for some
J\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
(\mathfrak{p},J)\models(1,a,\gamma,\delta)
. So, there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
and
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=p^{n}
if and only if there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
J_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},J_{i})\models(1,a,\gamma,\delta)
and
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=p^{n}
. The last statement is equivalent to
(p,n;\lambda,\gamma,a,\delta)\in X(R)
.
∎

Proposition 10.2
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain with
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
and
X(R)
recursive. There is an algorithm which, given
c\in R
,
C\in\mathbb{N}
and
\Xi
an auxiliary sentence, answers whether there exists
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that

M\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\Xi.

Proof.

Let
\chi
be the sentence
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\Xi
. By
2.11
, there exists
M\models{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}
if and only if there exist prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and uniserial
R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
-modules
U_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq l
such that
\oplus_{i=1}^{l}U_{i}\models{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}
. Moreover, we may assume that
U_{i}/U_{i}c\neq 0
for all
1\leq i\leq l
. By
6.3
, for each
1\leq i\leq l
, either
c\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}U_{i}
or, for some ideal
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
,
U_{i}\cong R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cI_{i}
. Thus, there exists
M\models{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}
if and only if there exist
A,B\in\mathbb{N}
with
AB=C
,
F\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that

F\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{x=0}}\right|=A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{c|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi

and
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=B
and
R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cI_{i}\models\Xi
for
1\leq i\leq h
. So, since
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
is recursive, by
7.6
,
it is enough to show that there is an algorithm which answers whether there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=B
and
R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cI_{i}\models\Xi
for
1\leq i\leq h
.

By
6.10
, we can compute
(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for
1\leq j\leq n
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq n
if and only if
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cI\models\Xi
. Thus there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals

I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=B
and
R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cI_{i}\models\Xi
for
1\leq i\leq h
if and only if there exist
B_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
for
1\leq j\leq n
such that
B=\prod_{j=1}^{n}B_{j}
and for
1\leq j\leq n
, there exist
h_{j}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{ij}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{ij}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}
for
1\leq i\leq h_{j}
such that
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h_{j}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\right|=B_{j}
and
(\mathfrak{p}_{ij},I_{ij})\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for
1\leq i\leq h_{j}
. The result now follows from
10.1
.
∎

Corollary 10.3
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain with
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
and
X(R)
recursive. There is an algorithm which, given
b\in R
,
B\in\mathbb{N}
and
\Xi
an auxiliary sentence, answers whether there exists
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that

M\models\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B\wedge\Xi.

Proof.

Apply
2.3
to
10.2
.
∎

Proposition 10.4
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain with
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
and
X(R)
recursive. There is an algorithm which, given
c,b\in R
,
C,B\in\mathbb{N}
and
\Xi
an auxiliary sentence, answers whether there exists
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that

M\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B\wedge\Xi.

Proof.

For
\alpha\in R
, we write
\alpha\notin\textnormal{Att}
for the sentence
\left|\nicefrac{{x\alpha=0}}{{x=x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\alpha|x}}\right|=1
. Recall,
2.9
, that for all
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
, there are
M_{1},M_{2}\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that
M\equiv M_{1}\oplus M_{2}
,
M_{1}
satisfies
\alpha\notin\textnormal{Att}
and
M_{2}
satisfies
\alpha-1\notin\textnormal{Att}
.

Let
\alpha,u,v\in R
be such that
c\alpha=bu
and
b(\alpha-1)=cv
. There exists an
R
-module which satisfies
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B\wedge\Xi
if and only if there exist
C_{1},C_{2},B_{1},B_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
with
C=C_{1}C_{2}
and
B=B_{1}B_{2}
and there exists an
R
-module satisfying

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{bu|x}}\right|=C_{1}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B_{1}\wedge\alpha\notin\textrm{Att}\wedge\Xi

and an
R
-module satisfying

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C_{2}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xcv=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B_{2}\wedge(\alpha-1)\notin\textrm{Att}\wedge\Xi.

By
2.3
, there exists an
R
-module satisfying

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C_{2}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xcv=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B_{2}\wedge\alpha\notin\textrm{Att}\wedge\Xi

if and only if there exists an
R
-module satisfying

\left|\nicefrac{{xc=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=C_{2}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{cv|x}}\right|=B_{2}\wedge\alpha\notin\textrm{Att}\wedge D\Xi.

Thus, in order to prove the proposition, it is enough to show that there is an algorithm which, given
b,u\in R
,
C,B\in\mathbb{N}
and
\Xi
an auxiliary sentence, answers whether there exists an
R
-module satisfying the sentence
\chi
defined as

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{bu|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B\wedge\Xi.

We may assume that
bu\neq 0
, for otherwise
\chi
is a sentence about an
R
-module of fixed finite size and since
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
is recursive, we can decide whether there exist
R
-modules satisfying such sentences.

By
2.11
and
6.3
, there exists an
R
-module satisfying
\chi
if and only if there exists
F\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
with
bu\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}F
,
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
and
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
with
Mbu=M
such that
F\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/buI_{i}\oplus M
satisfies
\chi
. Now, this happens if and only if there exist
C_{1},C_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
and
B_{1},B_{2},B_{3}\in\mathbb{N}
with
C=C_{1}C_{2}
and
B=B_{1}B_{2}B_{3}
such that

F\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{x=0}}\right|=C_{1}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B_{1}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{bu|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi,

\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/buI_{i}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{bu|x}}\right|=C_{2}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B_{2}\wedge\Xi\text{, and}

M\models\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B_{3}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{bu|x}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi.

In view of
7.6
and
10.3
, it is therefore enough to show that there is an algorithm which answers whether there exists
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that

\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/buI_{i}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{bu|x}}\right|=C_{2}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B_{2}\wedge\Xi.

By
6.10
, we can compute
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for
1\leq j\leq n
such that
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/buI\models\Xi
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq n
. It is therefore enough to show that there is an algorithm which, given
(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
,
b,u\in R
and
C,B\in\mathbb{N}
, answers whether there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq h
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
and

\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/buI_{i}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{bu|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B.

Case
r=0
:

In this case
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
implies
I=0
. Moreover
(\mathfrak{p},0)\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
if and only if
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}
and
\delta\neq 0
. Thus, there exist prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},0)\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
and
\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{bu|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B
if and only if
\delta\neq 0
,
B=1
and there exist prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
and
|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/buR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}|=C
. Such an algorithm exists since
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
is recursive.

Case
r\neq 0
:

For all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}
and ideals
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, since
bu\neq 0
,

\left|xb=0/x=0(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/buI)\right|=\left|(buI:b)/buI\right|=\left|I/bI\right|.

Now, if
\left|I/bI\right|
is finite but not equal to
1
then
I=\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}}
for some
\lambda\neq 0
and
\left|I/bI\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/bR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|
. Since
b\neq 0
,
\left|I/bI\right|=1
if and only if
b\notin I^{\#}
.

Therefore, there exist prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that

\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/buI_{i}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{bu|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=B\wedge\Xi
if and only if there exist
C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
with
C^{\prime}C^{\prime\prime}=C
such that the following conditions hold.

(i)

There exist prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and
\lambda_{i}\in R\backslash\{0\}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},\lambda_{i}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}})\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
,
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/buR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=C^{\prime}

and
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/bR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=B
.

(ii)

There exist prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta b)
and
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/buR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=C^{\prime\prime}
.

Note that, if
(\mathfrak{p},\lambda R_{\mathfrak{p}})\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
then
(\mathfrak{p},rR_{\mathfrak{p}})\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
. Since
(rR_{\mathfrak{p}})^{\#}=\mathfrak{p}R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
(\mathfrak{p},rR_{\mathfrak{p}})\models(r,ra,\gamma,\delta)
if and only if
\gamma\delta\notin\mathfrak{p}
. So
(i)
holds if and only if there exist prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq h^{\prime}
such that
\gamma\delta\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
and
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h^{\prime}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/buR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=C^{\prime}
and
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h^{\prime}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/bR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=B
. So, since
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
is recursive, by
7.2
, there is an algorithm which answers whether (i) holds or not.

By
10.1
, since
X(R)
is recursive, there is an algorithm which answers whether (ii) holds or not.
∎

The rest of this section is spent proving the following proposition.

Proposition 10.5
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain with
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
and
\mathrm{DPR}(R)
recursive. There is an algorithm which, given
c,b\in R
,
C,B\in\mathbb{N}
and
\Xi
