an auxiliary sentence, answers whether there exists
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that

M\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B\wedge\Xi.

We could choose the module in the following definition uniquely. For instance, one can show that when
\mathfrak{q}\supsetneq\mathfrak{p}
the uniserial module
[\mathfrak{q}R_{\mathfrak{q}}:\lambda\mathfrak{p}R_{\mathfrak{q}}]/\mathfrak{q}R_{\mathfrak{q}}
has the required theory where
[\mathfrak{q}R_{\mathfrak{q}}:\lambda\mathfrak{p}R_{\mathfrak{q}}]
is the set of elements
a\in Q
, the fraction field of
R
, such that
a\lambda\mathfrak{q}R_{\mathfrak{q}}\subseteq\mathfrak{q}R_{\mathfrak{q}}
. However, we are only ever interested in modules up to elementary equivalence.

Definition 10.6
.

For
\lambda\in R\backslash\{0\}
and prime ideals
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q}\lhd R
, let
M(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q},\lambda)
be
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/\lambda\mathfrak{q}R_{\mathfrak{p}}
if
\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}
and a module with theory dual, in the sense of
[
Her93
, 6.6]
, to the theory of
R_{\mathfrak{q}}/\lambda\mathfrak{p}R_{\mathfrak{q}}
if
\mathfrak{q}\supsetneq\mathfrak{p}
.

Lemma 10.7
.

Suppose that
\lambda,a,\gamma,\delta\in R
with
\lambda\neq 0
are such that if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(\lambda,\lambda a,\gamma,\delta)
then
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\models\Xi
and if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(\lambda,\lambda a,\delta,\gamma)
then
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\models D\Xi
. Then
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}
,
\delta\notin\mathfrak{q}
and
a\in\mathfrak{p}\cap\mathfrak{q}
implies
M(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q},\lambda)\models\Xi
.

Proof.

Let
(\lambda,\lambda a,\gamma,\delta)
be such that if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(\lambda,\lambda a,\gamma,\delta)
then
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\models\Xi
and if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(\lambda,\lambda a,\delta,\gamma)
then
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\models D\Xi
.

Suppose that
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}
,
\delta\notin\mathfrak{q}
and
a\in\mathfrak{p}\cap\mathfrak{q}
. If
\mathfrak{p}\supseteq\mathfrak{q}
then
(\mathfrak{p},\lambda\mathfrak{q}R_{\mathfrak{p}})\models(\lambda,\lambda a,\gamma,\delta)
. So
M(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q},\lambda)\models\Xi
. Now suppose that
\mathfrak{q}\supsetneq\mathfrak{p}
. Then
(\mathfrak{q},\lambda\mathfrak{p}R_{\mathfrak{q}})\models(\lambda,\lambda a,\delta,\gamma)
. So
R_{\mathfrak{q}}/\lambda\mathfrak{p}R_{\mathfrak{q}}\models D\Xi
. Hence, by definition,
M(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q},\lambda)\models\Xi
.
∎

Lemma 10.8
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain with
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
recursive. There is an algorithm which, given
r,c\in R\backslash\{0\}
,
a,b,\gamma,\delta\in R
and
A,B,C\in\mathbb{N}
, answers whether there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})\models(1,a,\gamma,\delta)
,
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/I_{i}\right|=A
and

\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cI_{i}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
be a prime ideal and
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
be an ideal. Then
a\in I_{i}
if and only if
rca\in rcI_{i}
. So
a\in I_{i}
if and only if
\left|\nicefrac{{rca|x}}{{x=0}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/rcI)\right|=1
. If
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/rcI\neq 0
then
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}
if and only if
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\gamma|x}}\right|=1
, and,
\delta\notin I^{\#}
if and only if
\left|\nicefrac{{x\delta=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=1
. Note that

\left|\nicefrac{{rc|x}}{{x=0}}(\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/rcI_{i})\right|=\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/I_{i}\right|.

Therefore, there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
as in the statement if and only if there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/rcI_{i}
satisfies

{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}:=\left|\nicefrac{{rc|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=A\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B\\
\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{rca|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{\gamma|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{x\delta=0}}{{x=0}}\right|=1.

By
8.1
, there is an algorithm answering whether there exists
\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/rcI_{i}
satisfying
\chi
.
∎

Proposition 10.9
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain with
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
,
\mathrm{DPR}(R)
and
X(R)
recursive. There is an algorithm which, given
C,B\in\mathbb{N}
,
c,b\in R
with
c\neq 0
and
r,a,\gamma,\delta\in R
with
r\neq 0
, answers whether there exists
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
satisfying

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B,

such that
M
is a direct sum of

•

modules of the form
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/rcI
where
\mathfrak{p}
is prime,
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is an ideal and
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,a,\gamma,\delta)
, and,

•

modules of the form
M(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q},r)
where
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q}\lhd R
are prime ideals such that
\mathfrak{p}+\mathfrak{q}\neq R
,
c\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}
,
\delta\notin\mathfrak{q}
,
a\in\mathfrak{p}
and
a\in\mathfrak{q}
.

Proof.

Recall that,
3.7
, if
\text{DPR}(R)
is recursive then so is
\text{DPR}_{2}(R)
.

Case 1:

(c\gamma,\delta,a,a,a,b)\notin\text{DPR}_{2}(R)

There exist prime ideals,
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q}
with
\mathfrak{p}+\mathfrak{q}\neq R
such that
c\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}
,
\delta\notin\mathfrak{q}
,
a\in\mathfrak{p}
and
a,b\in\mathfrak{q}
. So,
|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(M(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q},r))|=1
, since
c\notin\mathfrak{p}
, and,
|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(M(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q},r))|>1
, since
b\in\mathfrak{q}
.

Therefore, there exists
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
as in the statement if and only if there exists
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
such that
(\mathfrak{p},I_{i})\models(1,a,\gamma,\delta)
for
1\leq i\leq h
and such that

\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/rcI_{i})\right|=C.

Since
X(R)
is recursive, we are done by
10.1
.

Case 2:

(c\gamma,\delta,a,a,a,b)\in\text{DPR}_{2}(R)

Then
|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(M(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q},r))|=1
and
|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(M(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q},r))|=1
for all primes
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q}
such that
\mathfrak{p}+\mathfrak{q}\neq R
,
c\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}
,
\delta\notin\mathfrak{q}
,
a\in\mathfrak{p}
and
a\in\mathfrak{q}
.

By
3.3
, there exist
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\epsilon,t,s_{1},s_{2}\in R
such that

(\epsilon\gamma c)^{n}=at\ \ \text{and}\ \ ((\epsilon-1)\delta)^{n}=as_{1}+bs_{2}.

For all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
, either
\epsilon\notin\mathfrak{p}
or
\epsilon-1\notin\mathfrak{p}
. Thus
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,a,\gamma,\delta)
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,a,\epsilon\gamma,\delta)
or
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,a,(\epsilon-1)\gamma,\delta)
.

Therefore, it is enough to be able to effectively answer whether there exist
C_{1},C_{2},B_{1},B_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
with
C_{1}\cdot C_{2}=C
,
B_{1}\cdot B_{2}\geq B
and
B_{1},B_{2}\leq B
, such that

(1)

there is a sum of modules of the form
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/rcI
where
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,a,\epsilon\gamma,\delta)
which satisfy
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C_{1}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B_{1}
, and

(2)

there is a sum of modules of the form
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/rcI
where
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,a,(\epsilon-1)\gamma,\delta)
which satisfy
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C_{2}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B_{2}
.

Suppose that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,a,\epsilon\gamma,\delta)
and
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/rcI)\right|\leq C_{1}
. Then

\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\right|\leq\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/atR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/(\epsilon\gamma c)^{n}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/c^{n}R_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|\leq\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|^{n}

because
a\in I
and
\gamma\epsilon\notin\mathfrak{p}
.
So,
\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/rcI)\right|\leq C_{1}
implies
\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/I\right|\leq C_{1}^{n}
. Therefore, there exists a sum of modules as in
(1)
if and only if there is
A\leq C_{1}^{n}
such that there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
with
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})\models(1,a,\epsilon\gamma,\delta)
,
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/I_{i}\right|=A
and

\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/rcI_{i}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C_{1}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B_{1}.

By
10.8
and
8.1
, there is an algorithm which answers this question.

Suppose that
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(1,a,(\epsilon-1)\gamma,\delta)
. Since
a\in I
, either
a\notin\mathfrak{p}
and
I=R_{\mathfrak{p}}
, or,
a\in I^{\#}
.
If
a\in I^{\#}
then, since
(\epsilon-1)\delta\notin I^{\#}
,
bs_{2}=((\epsilon-1)\delta)^{n}-as_{1}\notin I^{\#}
. So
b\notin I^{\#}
and hence
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/rcI)\right|=1
.

Thus, there exists a sum of modules as required in
(2)
if and only if there exist
C_{2}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
with
C_{2}=C_{2}^{\prime}C_{2}^{\prime\prime}
such that

(1)

there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{i},I_{i})\models(1,a,(\epsilon-1)\delta b,\gamma)
and

\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/rcI_{i}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C_{2}^{\prime}\text{, and}

(2)

there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
a\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
,
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
,
\delta\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
and

\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/rcR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C_{2}^{\prime\prime}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B_{2}.

Since for
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
prime and
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(R_{p}/rcI)\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|
, by
10.1
, there is an algorithm which answers whether (i) holds.

To conclude the proof we need to show that we can effectively answer whether (ii) holds or not. Let
\alpha,u,v\in R
be such that
b\alpha=rcu
and
rc(\alpha-1)=bv
. If
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}
then

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/crR_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|\ \ \text{ and }\ \ \left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/crR_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/crR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|.

If
\alpha-1\notin\mathfrak{p}
then

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/crR_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|\ \ \text{ and }\ \ \left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/crR_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=\left|R_{\mathfrak{p}}/bR_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|.

Since for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
, either
\alpha\notin\mathfrak{p}
or
\alpha-1\notin\mathfrak{p}
, by
7.3
, there is an algorithm which answers whether (ii) holds or not.
∎

Lemma 10.10
.

Let
R
be a recursive Prüfer domain with
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
and
\mathrm{DPR}(R)
recursive. There is an algorithm which, given
b,c,\gamma\in R
and
B,C\in\mathbb{N}
, answers whether there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
with
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that

\bigoplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B.

Proof.

We split the proof into
3
cases. Let
\chi
be the sentence

\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B.

Case
b\neq 0
:

Then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(R_{\mathfrak{p}})\right|=1
for all prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
. So

\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\models{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}
if and only if
B=1
and
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=C
. Since
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
is recursive, we are done by
7.3
.

Case
b=0
and
C>1
:

Then
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}})\right|=\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|
. So
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=C>1
implies
\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}})\right|
is infinite. So

\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\models{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}
if and only if
\left|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cR_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right|=C
. So, since
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
is recursive, we are done by
7.3
.

Case
b=0
and
C=1
:

If
B=1
then the zero module satisfies
\chi
i.e.
h=0
. Otherwise, if
\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\models{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}
and
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq h
then
h\geq 1
and
c\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq h
. So there exists a prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
such that
\gamma c\notin\mathfrak{p}
. Conversely, if
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
is a prime ideal such that
c\gamma\notin\mathfrak{p}
then
R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\models{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}
. There exists a prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
such that
\gamma c\notin\mathfrak{p}
if and only if
(\gamma c,1,0,0)\notin\mathrm{DPR}(R)
.
∎

Proof of
10.5
.

We may assume that
c\neq 0
since if
c=0
then
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B\wedge\Xi
is a statement about an
R
-module of a fixed finite size and in this case we know such an algorithm exists, by
7.6
, since
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
is recursive.

By
6.10
, we can compute
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})\in R^{4}
for
1\leq j\leq n
such that
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cI\models\Xi
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq n
and such that
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cI\models D\Xi
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},I)\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\delta_{j},\gamma_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq n
.

Claim:
There exists
M\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that

M\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B\wedge\Xi

if and only if there exist
C_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
for
0\leq j\leq n
and
B_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
,
B_{j}\leq B
for
0\leq j\leq n+1

with
\prod_{j=0}^{n}C_{j}=C
and
\prod_{j=0}^{n+1}B_{j}\geq B
,
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

There exists
F\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that

F\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{x=0}}\right|=C_{0}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B_{0}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{c|x}}{{x=0}}\right|=1\wedge\Xi.

(2)

There exists
M^{\prime}\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
such that

M^{\prime}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=1\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B_{n+1}\wedge\Xi.

(3)

For
1\leq j\leq n
,

(a)
j

if
r_{j}=0
then there exist
h_{j}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{ij}\lhd R
for
1\leq i\leq h_{j}
such that
\gamma_{j}\notin\mathfrak{p}_{ij}
,
\delta_{j}\neq 0
and

M_{j}:=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{h_{j}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C_{j}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B_{j},\text{ and}

(b)
j

if
r_{j}\neq 0
then there exist
h_{j},k_{j}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{j},\mathfrak{q}_{j},\mathfrak{p}_{ij}\lhd R
and ideals
I_{ij}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}
for
1\leq i\leq h_{j}
such that
(\mathfrak{p}_{ij},I_{ij})\models(1,a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for
1\leq i\leq h_{j}
,
\gamma_{j}\notin\mathfrak{p}_{j}
,
\delta_{j}\notin\mathfrak{q}_{j}
,
a\in\mathfrak{p}_{j}
,
a\in\mathfrak{q}_{j}
, and

M_{j}:=M(\mathfrak{p}_{j},\mathfrak{q}_{j},r_{j})^{k_{j}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{h_{j}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/r_{j}cI_{ij}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C_{j}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B_{j}.

Proof of claim.

(
\Rightarrow
) By
6.3
, if
U
is a uniserial module with
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(U)
finite but non-zero then either
c\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}U
or
U\cong R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cI
for some prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\lhd R
and ideal
I\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Therefore, by
2.11
, there exists

M\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B\wedge\Xi

if and only if there exists
F\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
with
c\in\textnormal{ann}_{R}F
, prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\lhd R
and ideals
J_{i}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
and
M^{\prime}\in\textnormal{Mod}\text{-}R
with
\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(M^{\prime})\right|=1
such that

F\oplus M^{\prime}\oplus\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cJ_{i}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=C\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq B\wedge\Xi.

Since
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/cJ\models\Xi
if and only if
(\mathfrak{p},J)\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq n
, we may rewrite
\oplus_{i=1}^{h}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}/cJ_{i}
as
\oplus_{j=1}^{n}\oplus_{i=1}^{h_{j}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/cJ_{ij}
where
(\mathfrak{p}_{ij},J_{ij})\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for
1\leq j\leq n
and
1\leq i\leq h_{j}
.

If
r_{j}=0
then
(\mathfrak{p}_{ij},J_{ij})\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
if and only if
J_{ij}=0
,
\delta_{j}\neq 0
and
\gamma_{j}\notin\mathfrak{p}_{ij}
. If
r_{j}\neq 0
then
(\mathfrak{p}_{ij},J_{ij})\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
if and only if there exists
I_{ij}\lhd R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}
such that
J_{ij}=rI_{ij}
and
(\mathfrak{p}_{ij},I_{ij})\models(1,a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
.

Let
C_{0}:=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(F)\right|=\left|F\right|
and

B_{0}:=\min\{\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(F)\right|,B\}
. Let
B_{n+1}:=\min\{\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(M^{\prime})\right|,B\}
. If
r_{j}=0
then let

C_{j}:=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(\oplus_{i=1}^{h_{j}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}})\right|
and
B_{j}:=\min\{\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(\oplus_{i=1}^{h_{j}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}})\right|,B\}
.
If
r_{j}\neq 0
then let
C_{j}:=\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}(\oplus_{i=1}^{h_{j}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/r_{j}cI_{ij})\right|
and
B_{j}:=\min\{\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}(\oplus_{i=1}^{h_{j}}R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/r_{j}cI_{ij})\right|,B\}
. Now, setting
k_{j}=0
for
1\leq j\leq n
, we are done.

(
\Leftarrow
) Fix
1\leq j\leq n
. Suppose
r_{j}=0
. Then
(\mathfrak{p}_{ij},0)\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for each
1\leq i\leq h_{j}
and hence
R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}\models\Xi
. Suppose
r_{j}\neq 0
. Then
(\mathfrak{p}_{ij},r_{j}I_{ij})\models(r_{j},r_{j}a_{j},\gamma_{j},\delta_{j})
for each
1\leq i\leq h_{j}
and hence
R_{\mathfrak{p}_{ij}}/r_{j}cI_{ij}\models\Xi
.

By
10.7
,
\gamma_{j}\notin\mathfrak{p}_{j}
,
\delta_{j}\notin\mathfrak{q}_{j}
,
a\in\mathfrak{p}_{j}
and
a\in\mathfrak{q}_{j}
implies that
M(\mathfrak{p}_{j},\mathfrak{q}_{j},r_{j})\models\Xi
.
Thus

F\oplus M^{\prime}\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}M_{j}\models\Xi.

Therefore

F\oplus M^{\prime}\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}M_{j}\models\left|\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c|x}}\right|=\prod_{j=0}^{n}C_{i}\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{x=0}}\right|\geq\prod_{j=0}^{n+1}B_{i}\wedge\Xi.

Since
C=\prod_{j=0}^{n}C_{i}
and
\prod_{j=0}^{n+1}B_{i}\geq B
, we are done.

Since the set of
C_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
for
0\leq j\leq n
and
B_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
for
0\leq j\leq n+1
satisfying
(1)
is finite, it is enough to show that for fixed
C_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
for
0\leq j\leq n
and
B_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
for
0\leq j\leq n+1
, there are algorithms answering whether
(2),(3)
and
(4)
hold. By
7.6
, since
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
is recursive, there is an algorithm which answers whether
(2)
holds. By
3.9
, since
\mathrm{DPR}(R)
is recursive there is an algorithm which answers whether
(3)
holds. Since
\mathrm{DPR}(R),\mathrm{EPP}(R)
and
X(R)
are recursive, by
10.9
, if
r_{j}\neq 0
then there is an algorithm which answers whether (b)
j
holds. Since
\mathrm{DPR}(R)
and
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
are recursive, by
10.10
, if
r_{j}=0
then there is an algorithm which answers whether (a)
j
holds.
∎

11.
The main theorem

Theorem 11.1
.

Let
R
be a Prüfer domain. The theory of
R
-modules is decidable if and only if
\mathrm{DPR}(R)
,
\mathrm{EPP}(R)
and
X(R)
are recursive.

Proof.

The forward direction follows from
[
GLPT18
, 6.4]
(or
3.5
),
3.18
and
3.22
.

By
5.2
, in order to show that
T_{R}
is decidable, it is enough to show that there is an algorithm which, given a sentence
\chi
of the form

\left|\nicefrac{{d|x}}{{x=0}}\right|\square_{1}D\wedge\left|\nicefrac{{xb=0}}{{c|x}}\right|\square_{2}E\wedge{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{f,g}\wedge\Xi,

where
\square_{1},\square_{2}\in\{\geq,=,\emptyset\}
,
d,c,b\in R\backslash\{0\}
,
D,E\in\mathbb{N}
,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
,
g:Y\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
,
X,Y
are finite sets of pp-
1
-pairs of the form
\nicefrac{{xb^{\prime}=0}}{{x=0}}
or
\nicefrac{{x=x}}{{c^{\prime}|x}}
and
\Xi
is an auxiliary sentence, answers whether there exists an
R
-module which satisfies
\chi
or not.

Let
W
and
V
be as in §
9
. By
9.5
,
9.7
,
9.9
and
9.11
, there is an algorithm which given
w\in W
reducible, returns
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{W}
such that
w\in V
if and only if
\underline{w}\in\mathbb{V}
, and,
\mathtt{exsig}\,\underline{w}<\mathtt{exsig}\,w
. Since the set of extended signatures is artinian, it is enough to show that there is an algorithm which, given
w\in W
not reducible, answers whether
w\in V
or not. By
9.13
and the statement just before that, it is enough to show that there is an algorithm which, given
w\in W
with extended signature in

S:=\{((\emptyset,\emptyset),(z_{1},z_{2}),(z_{3},z_{4}))\ |\ z_{1}+z_{2}\leq 1\text{ and }z_{3}+z_{4}\leq 1\},

answers whether
w\in V
or not. Now,
w\in V
if and only if
Dw\in V
. So, by
9.3
, we can reduce
S
further to

S:=\{((\emptyset,\emptyset),(1,0),(1,0)),((\emptyset,\emptyset),(1,0),(0,0)),((\emptyset,\emptyset),(1,0),(0,1))\}.

By
9.13
,
10.2
,
10.4
and
10.5
, such an algorithm exists.
∎

12.
Integer-valued polynomials

We use our main theorem,
11.1
, to show that the theory of modules over the ring of integer valued polynomials with rational valued coefficients,
\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
, is decidable.

First we fix some notation: For all
p\in\mathbb{P}
,
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
denotes
\mathbb{Z}
localised at the ideal generated by
p
,
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
denote the field of
p
-adic numbers,
v_{p}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{\infty\}
denotes the
p
-adic valuation on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
denote the
p
-adic integers.

The ring
\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
is the subring of
\mathbb{Q}[x]
consisting of all polynomials
a\in\mathbb{Q}[x]
such that
a(\mathbb{Z})\subseteq\mathbb{Z}
. Recall,
[
CC97
, I.1.1]
, that the polynomials

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}x\\
n\\
\end{array}\right):=\frac{x(x-1)\ldots(x-(n-1))}{n!}

are a basis for
\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
as a
\mathbb{Z}
-module. This readily gives us a recursive presentation of
\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
. The ring
\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
is a Prüfer domain
[
CC97
, VI.1.7]
.

Note,
[
CC97
, I.2.1]
, that, for all
p\in\mathbb{P}
, if
f\in\mathbb{Q}[x]
and
f(\mathbb{Z})\subseteq\mathbb{Z}
then
f(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
. Further, for any
p\in\mathbb{P}
, by continuity of polynomials over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
f(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
implies
f(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})\subseteq\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
.

The prime spectrum of
\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
is described in
[
CC97
, V.2.7]
. We recall the information we need.

•

For any
p\in\mathbb{P}
, the prime ideals of
\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
containing
p
are in bijective correspondence with the elements of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
by mapping
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
to

\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}:=\{f\in\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})\ |\ f(\alpha)\in p\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\}.

The prime ideals
\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
are exactly the maximal ideals of
\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
and the quotient
\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})/\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
has size
p
.

•

The non-zero prime ideals
\mathfrak{p}
of
\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
such that
\mathbb{Z}\cap\mathfrak{p}=\{0\}
are in bijective correspondence with the monic irreducible polynomials
q\in\mathbb{Q}[x]
via the mapping

q\mapsto\mathfrak{p}_{q}:=q\mathbb{Q}[x]\cap\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z}).

Note that
\mathfrak{p}_{q}\subseteq\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
if and only if
q(\alpha)=0
in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

It will sometimes be useful to have some alternate notation for the non-maximal prime ideals. For
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, let

\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathfrak{p}_{q},&\hbox{if $\alpha$ is algebraic and $q\in\mathbb{Z}[x]$ is its monic minimal polynomial;}\\
\{0\},&\hbox{if $\alpha$ is transcendental.}\end{array}\right.

This notation has the disadvantage that
\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}=\mathfrak{p}_{\beta}
does not imply
\alpha=\beta
. However, it allows us to work with
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
algebraic and transcendental uniformly in the following ways: Firstly, for
a\in\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
and
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
,
a\in\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}
if and only if
a(\alpha)=0
. Secondly, for
\mathfrak{q}\lhd R
a prime ideal and
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
,
\mathfrak{q}\subseteq\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
if and only if
\mathfrak{q}=\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
,
\mathfrak{q}=\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}
or
\mathfrak{q}=\{0\}

By
11.1
, we need to show that
\mathrm{DPR}(\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z}))
,
\mathrm{EPP}(\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z}))
and
X(\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z}))
are recursive. In order to do this, we will use the fact,
[
Ax68
, Thm 17]
, that the common theory
T_{\text{adic}}
of the valued fields
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, as
p
varies, is decidable. We shall work in a two-sorted language
\mathcal{L}_{val}
of valued fields with a sort for the field
K
, a sort
\Gamma
for the value group extended by
\infty
and a function symbol
v:K\rightarrow\Gamma
which will be interpreted as
v_{p}
in each
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. For convenience, we add a constant symbol
1
to the value group sort
\Gamma
, which for each valued field
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
will be interpreted as the least strictly positive element of the value group.

Let
\mathcal{L}_{val}^{0}
be the set of sentences in
\mathcal{L}_{val}
. The sets

T_{adic}:=\{\varphi\in\mathcal{L}_{val}^{0}\ |\ \text{ for all }p\in\mathbb{P},\ \mathbb{Q}_{p}\models\varphi\}

and

\{\varphi\in\mathcal{L}_{val}^{0}\ |\ \text{ there exists }p\in\mathbb{P}\text{ such that }\mathbb{Q}_{p}\models\varphi\}

are recursive. Hence, since
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\models\varphi
if and only if
\mathbb{Q}_{q}\models v(p)=0\vee\varphi
for all
q\in\mathbb{P}
, the set

\{(p,\varphi)\in\mathbb{P}\times\mathcal{L}_{val}^{0}\ |\ \mathbb{Q}_{p}\models\varphi\}

is recursive.

Proposition 12.1
.

The set
\text{DPR}(\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z}))
is recursive.

Proof.

Let
a,b,c,d\in\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
. Then
(a,b,c,d)\in\text{DPR}(\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z}))
if and only if

(1)

for all
p\in\mathbb{P}
and
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
,
a\in\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
,
b\notin\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
,
c\in\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
or
d\notin\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
;

(2)

for all
p\in\mathbb{P}
and
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
,
a\in\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
,
b\notin\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
,
c\in\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}
or
d\notin\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}
;

(3)

for all
p\in\mathbb{P}
and
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
,
a\in\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
,
b\notin\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
,
c=0
or
d\neq 0
;

(4)

for all
p\in\mathbb{P}
and
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
,
a\in\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}
,
b\notin\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}
,
c\in\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
or
d\notin\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
;

(5)

for all
p\in\mathbb{P}
and
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
,
a=0
,
b\neq 0
,
c\in\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
or
d\notin\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
;

(6)

for all
q\in\mathbb{Q}[x]
irreducible and monic,
a\in\mathfrak{p}_{q}
,
b\notin\mathfrak{p}_{q}
,
c\in\mathfrak{p}_{q}
or
d\notin\mathfrak{p}_{q}
;

(7)

for all
q\in\mathbb{Q}[x]
irreducible and monic,
a\in\mathfrak{p}_{q}
,
b\notin\mathfrak{p}_{q}
,
c=0
or
d\neq 0
;

(8)

for all
q\in\mathbb{Q}[x]
irreducible and monic,
a=0
,
b\neq 0
,
c\in\mathfrak{p}_{q}
or
d\notin\mathfrak{p}_{q}
; and

(9)

a=0
or
b\neq 0
or
c=0
or
d\neq 0

Define
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{1},{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{2},{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{3},{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{4},{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{5}\in\mathcal{L}^{0}_{val}
to be

{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{1}:=\forall x\ (v(x)<0\vee v(a(x))\geq 1\vee v(b(x))=0\vee v(c(x))\geq 1\vee v(d(x))=0),

{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{2}:=\forall x\ (v(x)<0\vee v(a(x))\geq 1\vee v(b(x))=0\vee c(x)=\vee d(x)\neq 0),

{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{3}:=\forall x\ (v(x)<0\vee v(a(x))\geq 1\vee v(b(x))=0),

{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{4}:=\forall x\ (v(x)<0\vee a(x)=0\vee b(x)\neq 0\vee v(c(x))\geq 1\vee v(d(x))=0),

and

{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{5}:=\forall x\ (v(x)<0\vee v(c(x))\geq 1\vee v(d(x))=0).

Claim:

(a,b,c,d)\in\mathrm{DPR}(\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z}))
if and only if

(1)

{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{1},{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{2},{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{4}\in T_{adic}
,

(2)

either
c=0
,
d\neq 0
or
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{3}\in T_{adic}
,

(3)

either
a=0
,
b\neq 0
or
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{5}\in T_{adic}
,

(4)

ac\in\textnormal{rad}_{\mathbb{Q}[x]}(b\mathbb{Q}[x]+d\mathbb{Q}[x])
,

(5)

a\in\textnormal{rad}_{\mathbb{Q}[x]}(b\mathbb{Q}[x])
or
c=0
or
d\neq 0
,

(6)

either
c=0
,
d\neq 0
or
c\in\textnormal{rad}_{\mathbb{Q}[x]}(d\mathbb{Q}[x])
, and

(7)

either
a=0
,
b\neq 0
,
c=0
or
d\neq 0
.

Recall that
a\in\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
if and only if
v_{p}(a(\alpha))\geq 1
and
a\in\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}
if and only if
a(\alpha)=0
. So, for
j\in\{1,2,4\}
,
(j)
holds if and only if
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{j}\in T_{adic}
,
(3)
holds if and only if
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{3}\in T_{adic}
,
c=0
and
d\neq 0
and, and
(5)
holds if and only if
{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\chi$}}_{5}\in T_{adic}
,
a=0
and
b\neq 0
.

The statement that, for all
\mathfrak{p}
such that
\mathfrak{p}=0
or
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{p}_{q}
with
q\in\mathbb{Q}[x]
monic irreducible,

a\in\mathfrak{p}\vee c\in\mathfrak{p}\vee b\notin\mathfrak{p}\vee d\notin\mathfrak{p}

is equivalent to
ac\in\text{rad}_{\mathbb{Q}[x]}(b\mathbb{Q}[x]+d\mathbb{Q}[x])
.
So
(6)
and
(9)
hold if and only if (iv) holds. Similarly,
(7)
and
(9)
holds if and only if (v) holds and,
(8)
and
(9)
holds if and only if (vi) holds. Finally
(9)
holds if and only if (vii) holds. So the claim holds.

Since
T_{adic}
is decidable, we can effectively decide whether
(i),(ii)
and
(iii)
hold. If
a,b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Q}[x]
then
a\in\textnormal{rad}_{\mathbb{Q}[x]}(b_{1}\mathbb{Q}[x]+b_{2}\mathbb{Q}[x])
if and only if for all
q\in\mathbb{Q}[x]
irreducible,
q
divides
b_{1}
and
q
divides
b_{2}
implies
q
divides
a
. Since
\mathbb{Q}
has a splitting algorithm, there is an algorithm which, given
a,b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{Q}[x]
, decides whether
a\in\textnormal{rad}_{\mathbb{Q}[x]}(b_{1}\mathbb{Q}[x]+b_{2}\mathbb{Q}[x])
. Therefore, we can effectively decide whether
(iv)
-
(vi)
holds. It is obvious that we can effectively decide whether
(vii)
holds.
∎

In order to analyse
\mathrm{EPP}(\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z}))
, we need to understand the valuation overrings of
\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
. For
p\in\mathbb{P}
, let
v_{p}:\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{\infty\}
denote the standard valuation on
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

•

For each
p\in\mathbb{P}
and
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
transcendental, define
v_{p,\alpha}:\mathbb{Q}(x)\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\cup\{\infty\}
by setting
v_{p,\alpha}(f/g)=v_{p}(f(\alpha)/g(\alpha))
.

•

For each
p\in\mathbb{P}
and
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
algebraic with monic minimal polynomial
q\in\mathbb{Z}[x]
, define
v_{p,\alpha}:\mathbb{Q}(x)\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}\cup\{\infty\}
by setting
v_{p,\alpha}(h)=(k,v_{p}(f(\alpha)/g(\alpha)))
where
h=q^{k}\cdot f/g
,
f(\alpha)\neq 0
and
g(\alpha)\neq 0
.

By
[
CC97
, VI.1.9]
,
\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}}
is the valuation ring of
v_{p,\alpha}
.

Let
e\in\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
and
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

•

For
p\in\mathbb{P}
and
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
transcendental,
v_{p,\alpha}(e)=v_{p}(e(\alpha))=N
if and only if

|\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}}/e\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}}|=p^{N}.

•

For
p\in\mathbb{P}
and
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
algebraic,
v_{p,\alpha}(e(\alpha))=N
if and only if

v_{p,\alpha}(e)=(0,N)
if and only if

|\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}}/e\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}}|=p^{N}.

Proposition 12.2
.

The set
\mathrm{EPP}(\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z}))
is recursive.

Proof.

Claim:

(p,M;a;\gamma;e,N)\in\mathrm{EPP}_{1}(Int(\mathbb{Z}))
if and only if
there exist
h\in\{1,\ldots,N+M\}
,
N_{i},M_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
for
1\leq i\leq h
with
\sum_{i=1}^{h}N_{i}=N
and
\sum_{i=1}^{h}M_{i}=M
such that for each
1\leq i\leq h
,

\mathbb{Q}_{p}\models\exists x\ v(x)\geq 0\wedge v(e(x))=N_{i}\wedge v(a(x))\geq M_{i}\wedge v(\gamma(x))=0.

Suppose
(p,M;a;\gamma;e,N)\in\mathrm{EPP}_{1}(Int(\mathbb{Z}))
. There exists
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{h}
and
l_{1},\ldots,l_{h}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that

\gamma\notin\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}
,
a\in\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}^{l_{i}}
,

p^{\sum_{i=1}^{h}l_{i}}=\prod_{i=1}^{h}|\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}}/\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}^{l_{i}}|=p^{M}

and

p^{\sum_{i=1}^{h}v_{p}(e(\alpha_{i}))}=\prod_{i=1}^{h}|\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}}/e\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}}|=p^{N}.

We may assume that
h\leq N+M
since the size of the set of
1\leq i\leq h
such that
v_{p}(e(\alpha_{i}))>0
or
l_{i}>0
is at most
N+M
. Set
N_{i}:=v_{p}(e(\alpha_{i}))
and
M_{i}:=l_{i}
. Then
N=\sum_{i=1}^{h}N_{i}
and
M=\sum_{i=1}^{h}M_{i}
. Since
a\in\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}^{M_{i}}
,
v_{p}(a(\alpha_{i}))\geq M_{i}
and since
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}
,
v_{p}(\gamma(\alpha_{i}))=0
, as required.

Conversely, suppose that there exist
h\in\{1,\ldots,N+M\}
,
N_{i},M_{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
for
1\leq i\leq h
with
\sum_{i=1}^{h}N_{i}=N
and
\sum_{i=1}^{h}M_{i}=M
such that for each
1\leq i\leq h
,

\exists x\ v(e(x))=N_{i}\wedge v(a(x))\geq M_{i}\wedge v(\gamma(x))=0

holds in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Let
\alpha_{i}
witness the truth of the sentence for each
1\leq i\leq h
. Set
\mathfrak{p}_{i}:=\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
and
I_{i}:=\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}^{M_{i}}
. Then
v_{p}(a(\alpha_{i}))\geq M_{i}
implies
a\in\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}^{M_{i}}
. Since
v_{p}(\gamma(\alpha_{i}))=0
,
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}
and
v_{p}(e(\alpha_{i}))=N_{i}
implies

|\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}}/e\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}}|=p^{N_{i}}.

So
(p,M;a;\gamma;e,N)\in\mathrm{EPP}(Int(\mathbb{Z}))
as required.

The proposition now follows from the claim since the set of
(p,\varphi)\in\mathbb{P}\times\mathcal{L}^{0}_{val}
such that
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\models\varphi
is recursive.
∎

Proposition 12.3
.

The set
X(\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z}))
is recursive.

Proof.

Claim:

(p,n;e,\gamma,a,\delta)\in X(R)
if and only if there exist
h\in\{0,\ldots,n\}
and
\alpha_{i}\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{h}v_{p}(e(\alpha_{i}))=n
,
v_{p}(\gamma(\alpha_{i}))=0
and either

(1)

v_{p}(\delta(\alpha_{i}))=0
,

(2)

a(\alpha_{i})=0
and
\delta(\alpha_{i})\neq 0
, or

(3)

a=0
and
\delta\neq 0
.

Recall,
3.21
,
(p,n;\lambda,\gamma,a,\delta)\in X(R)
if and only if there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}
and maximal ideals
\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that

p^{\sum_{i=1}^{h}v_{p}(e(\alpha_{i}))}=|\oplus_{i=1}^{h}\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}}/e\text{Int}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}}|=p^{n},

\gamma\notin\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}
and for each
1\leq i\leq h
, either
\delta\notin\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}
or there exists
\mathfrak{q}_{i}
a prime ideal such that
\mathfrak{q}_{i}\subsetneq\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}
,
a\in\mathfrak{q}_{i}
and
\delta\notin\mathfrak{q}_{i}
.

If
\mathfrak{q}
is a prime ideal strictly contained in
\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
then
\mathfrak{q}=\mathfrak{p}_{\alpha}
or
\mathfrak{q}=\{0\}
. Thus there exists
\mathfrak{q}\subsetneq\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
such that
a\in\mathfrak{q}
and
\delta\notin\mathfrak{q}
if and only if
a(\alpha)=0
and
\delta(\alpha)\neq 0
, or,
a=0
and
\delta\neq 0
. For
\alpha\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
,
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha}
if and only if
v_{p}(\gamma(\alpha))=0
.

Therefore
(p,n;e,\gamma,a,\delta)\in X(R)
if and only if there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}
and maximal ideals
\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq h
such that
n=\sum_{i=1}^{h}v_{p}(e(\alpha_{i}))
,
\gamma\notin\mathfrak{m}_{p,\alpha_{i}}
and for each
1\leq i\leq h
, either
v_{p}(\delta(\alpha_{i}))=0
, or,
a(\alpha)=0
and
\delta(\alpha)\neq 0
, or,
a=0
and
\delta\neq 0
. So we have proved the claim.

The right hand side of the claim can be written as a sentence
\varphi\in\mathcal{L}^{0}_{val}
so that
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\models\varphi
if and only if
(p,n;\lambda,\gamma,a,\delta)\in X(R)
. By convention
(p,0;\lambda,\gamma,a,\delta)\in X(R)
for all
p,\lambda,\gamma,a,\delta
. So, since the set of
(p,\varphi)\in\mathbb{P}\times\mathcal{L}^{0}_{val}
such that
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\models\varphi
is recursive, we are done.
∎

Theorem 12.4
.

The theory of modules of the ring of integer valued polynomials is decidable.

Proof.

This follows from
11.1
,
12.1
,
12.2
and
12.3
.
∎
